# Performance: reflection-heavy code under kiln (WP-551)

This report covers three compiles of a large reflection-heavy codebase under kiln. It
says where kiln's time goes in each, compares kiln with GCC 16.1 on the
in-tree shapes that model it, and proposes follow-up work. It
records measurements made on 2026-11-04 and is not regenerated. The bench
contract is [spec/bench.md](../../spec/bench.md), and the shapes are
generated by `cargo xtask gen-reflect` (S7 and S8 are new in WP-551). Each
follow-up row is a proposal and is not yet in the
backlog.

## Summary

- **No single hot spot.** No kiln function takes more than 8% of a stage
  by self cost. The cost is spread across the whole frontend, so a quick
  win of 10% or more of a stage does not exist and none is included.
  - The biggest common cost is allocator churn: `malloc`/`free` and their
    internals take 11–17% of self cost in every profile.
  - Next come two call-volume hot paths that run 60–190 M times:
    `TypeContext::view` and the redeclaration-chain walk
    (`DeclArena::redecl`, `Ast::first`).
  - Reflection-heavy compiles also pay for the Oven's interpreter dispatch
    loop: 18–25% of self cost.
- **Generator stage (`-freflection`).** This is the constexpr-heavy stage.
  kiln is fast here:
  - The Oven takes 44% of the 200-class generator compile. The rest is
    template instantiation and sema.
  - On the in-tree model (S7), kiln takes 0.31x GCC's time and 0.10x its
    memory at N=40.
  - kiln grows 2.4x in time and 1.7x in memory from N=40 to N=160. GCC
    grows 3.2x and 3.3x.
- **Production stage (`-std=c++20 -O2`).** This compile's growth is
  super-linear: 30.5 s at N=200, 148 s at N=600. The data points to LLVM
  (`opt` and `llc`), not to kiln's frontend. See
  [the production stage's growth](#the-production-stages-super-linear-growth).
  - The kiln++ process does about 12 s of the 30.5 s stage, scales close
    to linearly, and has no quadratic hot spot.
  - The rest is the `opt -O2`/`llc -O2` child processes. They are not in
    the profile, and their cost depends on how much inlining the one large
    binding function triggers.
  - On the in-tree model (S8), LLVM is 75–85% of the -O2 wall time, and
    the reported peak RSS is `llc`'s.
- **Two kiln correctness bugs make the IR handed to LLVM larger than
  clang's.** They are listed first in the follow-ups and affect run-time
  behaviour as well as compile time:
  - `if consteval` is taken as true in trial folds.
  - `noexcept(std::__relocate_a(...))` evaluates to false.

## Inputs and method

The profiles:

| Profile | What | Native | Callgrind Ir |
|---|---|--:|--:|
| R1 | Generator compile of the small fixture TU (`-freflection`) | — | 42.6 G |
| R2 | Generator compile of the 200-class synthetic TU (`-freflection`, N=200) | 18.3 s / 985 MB | 191.9 G |
| R3 | Production compile of the rendered 200-class binding TU (`-std=c++20 -O2`) | 30.5 s / 1.6 GB (148 s / 3.3 GB at N=600) | 118.5 G (kiln++ only) |
| S7 | In-tree `bench/reflect/s7` shape, N=100, syntax-only | 1.9 s | 18.7 G |
| S8 | In-tree `bench/reflect/s8` shape, N=100, syntax-only | 1.6 s | 15.5 G |

How the numbers were produced:

- **Builds.** All profiles use the same release kiln++ with debug info.
  R1–R3 were supplied. S7 and S8 were run here under
  `valgrind --tool=callgrind` with `ulimit -v 3000000`.
- **Callgrind's call graph is unreliable on this aarch64 host.**
  Inclusive costs come out inconsistent (children cost more than their
  parents, and recursion is folded wrongly). This report therefore uses
  callgrind only for **self cost, line attribution and call counts**,
  which are exact.
- **Phase split.** The phase table gives each function's self cost to a
  pipeline phase in one of two ways:
  - by crate and module path, for kiln functions;
  - by call-count-weighted propagation through the call-count edges, for
    libc and std code such as `malloc`/`free`/`memcpy`. These edges are
    reliable even though the inclusive costs are not.
- **True inclusive cost.** A frame-pointer sampling profiler (ptrace, 150
  Hz, symbolized with the binary's DWARF) produced the inclusive figures
  for S7 and S8 at N=400, and the growth ratios from N=100 to N=400.
- **The production profile R3 covers the kiln++ process only.** At -O2,
  kiln prints textual LLVM IR, pipes it to `opt -passes=default<O2>`, and
  then runs `llc -O2 -filetype=obj`. Those two child processes are not
  traced. Their share is estimated
  [below](#the-production-stages-super-linear-growth) from S8, where the
  three steps were timed separately.

## Phase split (inclusive, % of the kiln++ process's Ir)

Indented rows are a breakdown of the phase above them.

| Phase | R1 fixture gen | R2 200-class gen | R3 200-class prod | S7 | S8 |
|---|--:|--:|--:|--:|--:|
| lex + preprocess | 4.4 | 1.5 | 1.9 | 3.1 | 3.4 |
| parse | 4.7 | 0.9 | 0.7 | 1.4 | 2.5 |
| **sema** | **80.1** | **49.5** | **63.1** | **45.0** | **88.7** |
| &nbsp;&nbsp;template instantiation/deduction | 32.1 | 15.6 | 19.0 | 17.6 | 37.5 |
| &nbsp;&nbsp;declarations, redeclarations | 13.0 | 2.9 | 5.1 | 3.0 | 7.8 |
| &nbsp;&nbsp;overload resolution | 11.7 | 6.9 | 5.8 | 8.1 | 7.6 |
| &nbsp;&nbsp;conversions | 5.3 | 5.2 | 8.7 | 3.4 | 6.3 |
| &nbsp;&nbsp;class completion, special members | 4.6 | 1.8 | 5.8 | 1.5 | 4.5 |
| &nbsp;&nbsp;name lookup | 1.6 | 4.7 | 3.1 | 5.3 | 6.5 |
| &nbsp;&nbsp;expressions | 3.1 | 3.6 | 3.7 | 2.4 | 3.6 |
| &nbsp;&nbsp;reflection (meta) | 0.0 | 1.6 | 0.0 | 0.2 | 0.0 |
| **oven (constant evaluation)** | **7.6** | **43.9** | **10.7** | **50.5** | **5.3** |
| &nbsp;&nbsp;interpreter (vm) | 2.2 | 23.2 | 1.5 | 4.0 | 0.5 |
| &nbsp;&nbsp;bytecode compile | 2.2 | 7.5 | 6.3 | 2.0 | 2.7 |
| &nbsp;&nbsp;object memory model | 0.8 | 8.3 | 0.7 | 34.8 | 0.4 |
| &nbsp;&nbsp;type layout | 0.4 | 1.6 | 0.3 | 7.9 | 0.0 |
| &nbsp;&nbsp;value conversion | 0.6 | 1.3 | 0.9 | 0.0 | 0.6 |
| **codegen** | **3.0** | **4.1** | **23.4** | 0 | 0 |
| &nbsp;&nbsp;kiln_codegen (AST to IR) | 1.8 | 2.9 | 12.3 | 0 | 0 |
| &nbsp;&nbsp;kiln_llvm (printing textual IR) | 1.3 | 1.1 | 11.1 | 0 | 0 |

How to read it:

- **Generators.** A generator compile emits only a small driver, so
  codegen is minor.
  - The 200-class generator (R2) is the constexpr workload: the Oven takes
    44%, and half of that is interpreter dispatch.
  - The fixture generator (R1) is dominated by instantiating the
    codebase's headers.
- **The production compile (R3) uses no reflection.**
  - Its Oven share (10.7%) is almost all `try_fold` trial folding of
    ordinary expressions: integer folds, narrowing checks, `if`-condition
    truth, and `is_constant_initialized`.
    - About 575 k evaluations.
    - `compile_func` runs 168 k times, which is why bytecode compile (6.3%)
      outweighs execution (1.5%).
  - Codegen is 23%, and half of that is printing the textual IR that
    `opt` then has to parse again.

### S8's IR emission at -O2

S8 was also profiled at N=100 while emitting its -O2 IR, with no LLVM
passes run (`-O2 -S -emit-llvm -Xclang -disable-llvm-passes`). The run
counts 22.3 G Ir, and it has the same shape as R3.

| Phase | Share |
|---|--:|
| sema | 70.5% |
| codegen | 19.0% (kiln_codegen 9.5, kiln_llvm 9.6) |
| oven | 5.0% |
| lex + preprocess | 3.2% |
| parse | 2.0% |

The top self costs are `free` 9.0, `malloc` 5.4, `redecl` 4.5, `view`
4.3, `memcpy` 2.7, `_int_malloc` 2.0 and `DeclArena::get` 2.0.

`StrSearcher::new` also shows at 2.9%, but it is not part of a real -O2
compile:

- It comes from `lifetime_markers_llvm21`, which rewrites lifetime
  markers in the text that `-S` writes.
- That rewrite only runs for `-S` output at `-fclang-compat=22` with an
  older LLVM.
- It is not on the `-c` path.

## Hot functions by self cost

The percentages are of each profile's Ir. "Calls" is the number of calls
in that profile. Allocator rows (`free`, `malloc`, `_int_malloc`,
`_int_free`, `realloc`, `memcpy`) are libc's.

### R3: 200-class production compile

| # | Function | Self % | Calls | Diagnosis |
|--:|---|--:|--:|---|
| 1 | `free` | 7.95 | 99 M | About 99 M heap allocations per compile. 32% come from `Vec` growth (`finish_grow`, 32 M). |
| 2 | `kiln_ast::TypeContext::view` | 5.95 | 190 M | O(1), 37 Ir per call, called from everywhere: `canonical_view`, `as_record`, builtin-type tests. The cost is the volume. |
| 3 | `malloc` | 4.73 | 99 M | See row 1. |
| 4 | `kiln_ast::DeclArena::redecl` | 4.35 | 108 M | O(1), 48 Ir per call. Called by `Ast::first`, `template_redecl` and `definition` on every declaration query, and repeats work its callers already did. |
| 5 | `memcpy` | 3.18 | — | About 1.8% of the profile is `Sema::with_oven` moving the whole `Oven` out of and back into `Sema` by value. That is 414 Ir per call over 4.6 M calls (`oven_bridge.rs` `with_oven`, `self.oven.take()`). |
| 6 | `kiln_ast::DeclArena::get` | 1.94 | — | Bounds-checked arena read. The cost is the volume. |
| 7 | `kiln_ast::ExprArena::get` | 1.66 | — | As row 6. |
| 8 | `_int_malloc` | 1.51 | — | See row 1. |
| 9 | `ExprArena::children_into` | 1.19 | — | Child walks for dependence and error scans: `has_errors_in`, `expr_is_value_dependent`. |
| 10 | `DeclKind::payload` | 0.97 | — | Kind-to-payload decoding on every typed declaration accessor. |
| 11 | `RawVec::finish_grow` | 0.94 | 32 M | `Vec` growth without `with_capacity`. |
| 12 | `<String as fmt::Write>::write_char` | 0.91 | — | Textual IR printing, one character at a time: `kiln_llvm` name and constant printers. |
| 13 | `SipHasher13::write` | 0.91 | 17.8 M | std `HashMap` with `DefaultHasher` in `kiln_codegen`/`kiln_llvm` (name uniquing, constant tables). |
| 14 | `Sema::templ_link` | 0.90 | — | Template-to-pattern link lookup on every instantiation query. |
| 15 | `kiln_ast::Ast::first` | 0.79 | — | The first-declaration walk (see row 4). |

Further down: `as_record` 0.78, `run_loop` 0.76, `write_llvm_name` 0.72
(443 Ir per call), `Types::intern` 0.70, `SourceManager::find_range` 0.63
(268 Ir per call), `Mangler::try_sub` 0.41. The node-accounting helpers
`node_live_bytes`, `vec_rows` and `MemRow::of_map` together come to
about 1%. They run on every instantiation-frame push and pop and make
3 M allocations.

### R2: 200-class generator compile

| # | Function | Self % | Diagnosis |
|--:|---|--:|---|
| 1 | `kiln_oven::vm::Vm::run_loop` | 18.3 | The interpreter loop, 382 Ir per call. Cost is in the fetch/decode `match` and the bounds-checked `code[pc]`/register reads (`vm.rs` around lines 705–723). |
| 2 | `free` | 4.9 | Allocator churn, shared by sema and the Oven. |
| 3 | `TypeContext::view` | 4.1 | As in R3. |
| 4 | `DeclArena::redecl` | 3.6 | As in R3. |
| 5 | `malloc` | 2.9 | |
| 6 | `kiln_oven::mem::…::set_bits` | 2.8 | Mostly `Memory::copy` copying the initialized-bits map one byte at a time (`mem.rs` `Memory::copy`, `sp.set_init(dst+i, 1, v)` in a loop). |
| 7 | `Memory::check_storage` | 1.8 | Per-access lifetime and bounds check. |
| 8 | `memcpy` | 1.6 | |
| 9 | `Memory::store` | 1.3 | |
| 10 | `Types::extent_of` | 1.2 | Subobject extent recomputed per access. |
| 11 | `Vm::exec_builtin` | 1.1 | Reflection and string builtins. |
| 12 | `Sema::add_builtin_with` | 1.03 | Built-in operator candidate sets (4.6 M calls, 434 Ir per call), rebuilt per operator expression. |
| 13 | `Memory::alloc` | 0.9 | |
| 14 | `Memory::release` | 0.9 | |
| 15 | `SourceManager::find_range` | 0.9 | Location to file range binary search, for diagnostic and expansion locations. |

| # | Function | Self % | Diagnosis |
|--:|---|--:|---|
| 1 | `free` | 8.22 | |

### S7 and S8

- **S7 (N=100).** In order: `run_loop` 24.8, `free` 4.4,
  `view` 4.2, `Types::has_subobject` 3.0, `redecl` 2.8, `malloc` 2.6,
  `check_storage` 2.6, `Memory::union_path` 2.0, `store` 1.6,
  `extent_of` 1.5, `field_at` 1.4, `set_bits` 1.3.
  - Beyond the interpreter loop, the Oven's cost is the object model
    walking `std::string`'s small-string union: active-member checks,
    subobject paths and extents, recomputed for each access.
- **S8 (N=100).** In order: `free` 9.8, `malloc` 5.8, `view` 4.9,
  `redecl` 4.4, `memcpy` 2.9, `DeclArena::get` 2.0,
  `kiln_sema::profile_node` 1.7, `_int_malloc` 1.7. These are the same
  six functions as R3's first six.

### What grows faster than N (S8, N=100 to N=400)

From N=100 to N=400, S8's wall time grows 3.85x. These functions grow
faster than that:

| Function | Growth |
|---|--:|
| `fn_corresponds` | 19x |
| `deduce_call_arg` | 18x |
| `pick_prev_function` | 16.6x |
| `act_on_friend` | 14.5x |
| `friend_decl` | 13x (0.93 s, 11.5% at N=400) |
| `param_list` | 11.7x |
| `deduce_ty` | 9.5x |
| `TypeContext::lookup` | 7.4x |

The term behind the friend rows:

- It is quadratic.
- Every instantiation of the `std::tuple` hidden-friend operators checks
  redeclaration against every earlier friend of the same name.
  `pick_prev_function` loops over all candidates
  (`overload/redecl.rs`).
- It belongs to S8's shape and does not occur in the profiled codebase.
  R3 makes only 20 k `fn_corresponds` calls and 1.5 k `act_on_friend`
  calls, and R2 makes 155 k `fn_corresponds` calls.

## kiln vs GCC 16.1

### `cargo xtask bench bench/reflect --compare-with g++-16.1 --runs 3`

S7 and S8 were re-recorded on 2026-11-05 (WP-640) on a shared host with a
load average of about 10; S1–S6 below are from the run with a load of about 9.

| Entry | kiln ms / MB | GCC ms / MB | Time | Memory |
|---|--:|--:|--:|--:|
| s7 | 552.6 / 51.4 | 2282.7 / 468.7 | 0.24x | 0.11x |
| s8 | 699.0 / 77.4 | 892.4 / 283.0 | 0.78x | 0.27x |

The memory rise since the earlier rows (48.8 → 51.4 MB, 73.0 → 77.4 MB) was
already on main and does not come from WP-640. This was checked with five
back-to-back pairs per shape. Each pair runs main (f7cfd9a7c) and then WP-640,
or the reverse, one process at a time, with the same flags as the bench. The
load average was 8.6–9.2. Each cell gives the median of the five pairs, with
the range in brackets:

| Entry | main ms / MB | WP-640 ms / MB |
|---|--:|--:|
| s7 | 536.7 [521.4–550.9] / 49.0 | 536.8 [533.2–539.1] / 49.5 |
| s8 | 683.5 [682.1–710.5] / 77.4 | 687.9 [682.0–690.6] / 76.8 |

The WP-640 binary's baselines are in `bench/baselines/reflect.json` (load
6.3, `--runs 3`): s7 is 534.0 ms / 49.6 MB and s8 is 699.3 ms / 77.1 MB.

S1–S6 compile against GCC 16.1's libstdc++ `<meta>` since WP-638 (F3 and
F19). The same command, run on 2026-11-05 with a load average of about 9:

| Entry | kiln ms / MB | GCC ms / MB | Time | Memory |
|---|--:|--:|--:|--:|
| s1 | 499.8 / 50.8 | 735.0 / 150.8 | 0.68x | 0.34x |
| s2 | 708.8 / 64.7 | 754.5 / 193.9 | 0.94x | 0.33x |
| s3 | 487.1 / 37.6 | 1060.1 / 173.3 | 0.46x | 0.22x |
| s4 | 470.3 / 49.6 | 675.8 / 150.7 | 0.70x | 0.33x |
| s5 | 347.2 / 36.9 | 734.1 / 150.7 | 0.47x | 0.25x |
| s6 | 339.7 / 33.7 | 1702.9 / 437.7 | 0.20x | 0.08x |

`cargo xtask bench --scaling s<k> --runs 3` (fastest wall, largest RSS
in MiB). All six pass the gate: no ratio is above 4.6, and `peak_arena`
is flat for S1, S2, S4 and S5.

| Shape | N → 4N | kiln N | kiln 4N | kiln growth (time, RSS) | GCC N | GCC 4N | GCC growth |
|---|--:|--:|--:|--:|--:|--:|--:|
| S1 | 100 → 400 | 469 ms / 52.5 | 882 ms / 102.1 | 1.88x, 1.95x | 665 ms / 150.8 | 1710 ms / 299.3 | 2.57x, 1.98x |
| S2 | 50 → 200 | 686 ms / 65.4 | 1658 ms / 155.8 | 2.42x, 2.38x | 734 ms / 194.0 | 1896 ms / 458.3 | 2.58x, 2.36x |
| S3 | 100 → 400 | 448 ms / 37.6 | 854 ms / 41.0 | 1.91x, 1.09x | 1004 ms / 173.6 | 2943 ms / 381.1 | 2.93x, 2.20x |
| S4 | 100 → 400 | 464 ms / 53.4 | 846 ms / 101.7 | 1.82x, 1.91x | 629 ms / 150.8 | 1719 ms / 299.3 | 2.73x, 1.99x |
| S5 | 1000 → 4000 | 343 ms / 38.8 | 404 ms / 49.3 | 1.18x, 1.27x | 698 ms / 150.7 | 1950 ms / 306.6 | 2.79x, 2.03x |
| S6 | 500 → 2000 | 337 ms / 33.7 | 401 ms / 34.0 | 1.19x, 1.01x | 1660 ms / 438.0 | 5602 ms / 1451.5 | 3.37x, 3.31x |

kiln's `peak_arena` at N → 4N: S1 1464 → 1464, S2 4586 → 4586, S3
32200 → 127432, S4 1464 → 1464, S5 32272 → 32272, S6 4152 → 16440.

### S8 at `-c -O2`

| N | kiln total | IR emission (no passes) | `opt -O2` | `llc -O2` | GCC total |
|--:|--:|--:|--:|--:|--:|
| 40 | 5.72 s / 188 MB | — | — | — | 6.51 s / 464 MB |
| 100 | 11.05 s / 373 MB | 1.88 s / 187 MB | 5.32 s / 231 MB | 3.89 s / 372 MB | — |
| 160 | 19.67 s / 557 MB | 3.1 s | 8.97–9.23 s | 6.15 s | 18.51 s / 1209 MB |
| 400 | 46.61 s / 1319 MB | 6.89 s / 636 MB | 23.44 s / 741 MB | 15.78 s / 1318 MB | — |

Notes on this table:

- **Comparison with clang.** clang 19 compiles an annotation-stripped
  copy of S8 at N=160 in 16.14 s / 704 MB. kiln takes 18.07 s on the same
  file.
- **IR volume is the same as clang's.** At N=160, kiln emits 18521
  definitions and 34.7 MB of IR; clang emits 18475 definitions and
  37.4 MB. The largest function is `bind_all`, the one function that
  registers every class: 3311 instructions from kiln, 3591 from clang.
- **opt is about 20% slower on kiln's IR than on clang's.** On the same
  host, `opt -O2 -time-passes` takes 12.5 s on kiln's IR and 10.1 s on
  clang's. The difference is concentrated in a few passes:

  | Pass | kiln IR | clang IR |
  |---|--:|--:|
  | LoopVectorize | 0.46 s | 0.00 s |
  | IndVarSimplify | 0.27 s | 0.00 s |
  | SLPVectorizer | 0.47 s | 0.17 s |
  | GlobalOpt | 0.76 s | 0.47 s |
  | SimplifyCFG | 0.60 s | 0.41 s |

  The loop passes run because kiln's IR contains loops that clang's does
  not. Two kiln bugs cause them:
  - **`if consteval` / `std::is_constant_evaluated()` is true in runtime
    code** (F1). libstdc++'s `char_traits<char>::length` becomes a loop
    instead of `strlen`. The same goes for `char_traits::copy`, and
    `basic_string::_M_init_local_buf` zero-fills its buffer in a loop.
  - **`noexcept(std::__relocate_a(...))` is false** (F2).
    `vector::_M_realloc_append` takes the element-wise
    copy-and-destroy path (`__do_uninit_copy`, `_Destroy`) instead of
    relocation.
- **Erroneous-value zero-init in C++26.** kiln also emits 2905 `memset`s,
  where clang emits 166, because C++26 mode zero-initializes automatic
  variables (P2795R5, spec/codegen.md §7r). The production stage builds
  with `-std=c++20`, where this is off.

## The production stage's super-linear growth

The production compile goes from 30.5 s / 1.6 GB at N=200 to
148 s / 3.3 GB at N=600. That is 4.85x the time for 3x the classes.

1. **Most of the stage is LLVM, not kiln.**
   - R3 counts 118.5 G Ir for the kiln++ process alone.
   - Native rate: the syntax-only S8 run executes 15.5 G Ir in about
     1.63 s, roughly 9.5 G Ir/s. The production frontend is similar code
     (sema-dominated, with no Oven execution), so the same rate applies.
   - That puts the kiln++ process at about 12.5 s of the 30.5 s.
   - The other ~18 s (about 60%) is `opt` and `llc`, which run one after
     the other on kiln's output.
   - S8 at -O2 has the same split: LLVM is 75–85% of the wall time.
2. **kiln's frontend does not look quadratic.**
   - No function in R3 shows a super-linear signature:
     - The friend-redeclaration term that makes S8's frontend grow is
       absent (20 k calls).
     - The hottest functions are O(1) accessors whose call counts are
       proportional to declarations and instantiations.
   - S8's frontend grows 2.6x from N=40 to N=160 (this is the syntax-only
     scaling row). From N=100 to N=400 it grows 3.85x even with its
     quadratic friend term.
   - S8's IR emission grows 3.66x from N=100 to N=400.
   - If kiln++ scales linearly, it takes about 37 s at N=600. LLVM then
     takes about 110 s, about 6x its N=200 time for 3x the input.
3. **Why LLVM would grow faster than N.**
   - A rendered binding TU has one registration function whose size is
     proportional to N, plus N sets of small template instantiations that
     `opt -O2` inlines into it.
   - In S8, `opt` grows 4.4x and `llc` 4.1x for 4x N. The pass profile
     is flat in N except GlobalOpt and IPSCCP (6x for 4x N), which are
     module-wide passes over a growing global-constant set.
   - The reference TU's registration function is far larger than S8's.
     Inlining into it and the function-level passes that follow, such as
     GVN, instcombine and the register allocator in `llc`, are the
     expected super-linear terms.
   - This is the one claim the in-tree shapes cannot reproduce, because
     S8's `bind_all` stays at about 20 instructions per class.
4. **The peak RSS is `llc`'s.**
   - In every S8 run, the stage's peak equals `llc`'s peak.
   - The reference's 1.6 GB → 3.3 GB is 2x for 3x N. That fits a backend
     that holds the whole module, more than it fits the frontend
     (R3's frontend allocates about 99 M blocks but frees almost all of
     them).
5. **What kiln adds on top of LLVM.**
   - The textual IR round trip: printing is 11% of the frontend, and
     `opt`'s parse of 35 MB of text is 0.9 s at S8 N=160.
   - `opt` is about 20% slower on kiln's IR than on clang's, because of
     F1 and F2 above.
   - These are constant factors. They do not explain the growth.

**Confirming this needs one measurement on the reference TU** (follow-up
P1):

- kiln++ at N=200 and N=600 with the three steps timed separately:
  `-O2 -S -emit-llvm -Xclang -disable-llvm-passes`, then `opt -O2` and
  `llc -O2` on the output.
- The IR size and the instruction count of the largest function.
- `opt -time-passes` at both sizes.
- clang 19 `-O2` on the same TU for comparison.

If the growth is in `opt`'s inliner on the registration function, there
are three ways to address it:

- Split the registration function in the renderer.
- Mark the per-class registration as `noinline`/cold.
- Run kiln's own pipeline at `-O1` for this TU.

None of these is a kiln frontend change.

## Do the in-tree shapes reproduce the supplied profiles?

- **S7 models the 200-class generator compile (R2).**
  - It reproduces R2's top self-cost list: `run_loop` first, then
    allocator, `view`, `redecl`, and the Oven object model
    (`check_storage`, `set_bits`, `store`, `extent_of`).
  - It puts more weight on the object model (35% against 8%) because of
    `std::string`'s small-string union paths.
  - It puts less weight on interpreter dispatch and bytecode compile.
  - It does not model R2's header-driven template instantiation load.
- **S8 models the production compile (R3).**
  - Its first six self-cost functions are the same six as R3's, in nearly the same order.
  - It reproduces R3's template-instantiation dominance: end-of-TU
    instantiation is 91% inclusive.
  - Its IR at -O2 is as large as clang's.
  - It adds a quadratic hidden-friend term that the reference does not
    have.
  - It does not reproduce the reference's super-linear LLVM time.
- **Neither shape models the fixture TU's macro-heavy header
  preprocessing.** R1 has 249 k macro expansions and 7.7 M
  `Keywords::get` calls.

## Quick win

None. The brief allows one change of at most 150 lines that is clearly
safe and saves 10% or more of a stage. Nothing qualifies:

- The largest single kiln-owned cost is R2's `run_loop` at 18% self.
  Speeding up the dispatch loop safely is not a 150-line change.
- The largest one-place fix is the `with_oven` move, about 1.8% of R3.
- The `if consteval` bug (F1) would shrink the IR, but it is a semantic
  change to the Oven's bytecode compiler: the compiled function is shared
  between constant and trial contexts, and call memoization depends on
  it. It needs its own WP and conformance tests.

## `synth_accessor_god` production TU (WP-633)

This is the profiled codebase's stress slug: one class with a very large
number of accessor member functions, each bound one by one through a
lambda wrapper and a class-template `def` chain. Its production TU is plain
C++20 compiled against libstdc++ 16.1 with `-O2 -DNDEBUG -fPIC
-fvisibility=hidden -c`. The workload's harness measured 263.8 s and
1.08 GB on kiln, against 75.5 s and 2.43 GB for g++ 16.1 (3.5×). Every
other slug is at most 1.02×. The measurements below were taken on
2026-11-05 through the captured harness command. Run-to-run variance on
this host is about ±30%, so each before/after pair was taken back to
back.

### Split of the real TU

"Before" is the harness-profile kiln++ at main bd696efeb. "After" adds the
two WP-633 changes below. The `-c` row runs the driver's own pipeline
(`opt -passes=default<O2>`, then `llc -O2`) through a timing wrapper that
records each tool's wall time and peak RSS.

| Stage | Before wall | Before peak RSS | After wall | After peak RSS |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| parse + sema (`-fsyntax-only`) | 8.01 s | 459,948 KB | 9.21 s | 460,620 KB |
| + codegen + IR print (`-emit-llvm -o /dev/null`, no IR passes) | 12.75 s | 1,044,128 KB | 14.07 s | 876,556 KB |
| full `-c` | 334.75 s | 1,078,920 KB | 310.96 s | 1,083,824 KB |
| … of which `opt` | 306.48 s | 702,724 KB | 281.37 s | 702,964 KB |
| … of which `llc` | 15.93 s | 1,078,920 KB | 15.58 s | 1,083,824 KB |

The IR is byte-identical before and after:

- 162,939,973 bytes;
- 35,431 definitions and 18,272 comdats;
- 1,771,581 instructions.

The object file is 17,500,448 bytes. One function holds 92,547 of those
instructions: the TU's registration function, with 17,221 basic blocks,
8,613 invokes, 6,455 landing pads and 10,762 allocas. All the other
functions are under 1,000 instructions.

The kiln frontend is 14 s of the 311 s. The rest is LLVM.

### Where `opt` spends it

`-time-passes-per-run` on the real TU reports 226.4 s of pass time. Two
single pass runs on the registration function account for 91.6% of it:

- one `JumpThreadingPass` run: 52.9%;
- one `MemCpyOptPass` run: 38.7%.

The aggregated `-time-passes` run agrees: JumpThreading is 64.8% and
MemCpyOpt is 29.8%. Both passes are super-linear in the size of one
function with many landing pads and allocas. Smaller functions are not
the problem: every other function together costs about 8%.

The other compilers on the same TU:

- **clang 19:** 432.52 s and 1,345,980 KB in total. Of that, 417.9 s is
  codegen, with JumpThreading at 254.7 s and MemCpyOpt at 123.8 s. On this
  shape kiln's IR costs LLVM no more than clang's own IR does.
- **LLVM 22** `opt`/`llc` on kiln's IR: 412.73 s in total (`opt` 375.40 s,
  `llc` 21.62 s). Time moves from JumpThreading to MemCpyOpt (43.2%), LICM
  (39.9%) and LoopRotate (11.6%).

Neither is faster.

The obvious LLVM limits do not help either. The run used
`-jump-threading-threshold=0 -jump-threading-implication-search-threshold=0
-memssa-check-limit=10` on the after binary and gave these results:

- `-c` total: 293.61 s;
- `opt`: 265.93 s;
- JumpThreading: 158.7 s, 60.8% of pass time;
- MemCpyOpt: 84.2 s, 32.3%.

The cost is not in the duplication or the clobber-walk budgets these flags cap.
It is in the per-function analysis work that both passes repeat over
17 k blocks. Lazy value info and dominator-tree updates are the likely
source, but that is not isolated. Since no pass flag helps, the pipeline was
not changed.

GCC compiles the same TU in 75.5 s. The difference is in the optimizer's
treatment of one huge function: GCC's pass set at `-O2` is linear enough
here, and LLVM's is not. It is not in the amount of work kiln hands the
optimizer.

### Synthetic reproducer and scaling

The reproducer is clean-room. It has one class with 100·K accessor pairs,
each bound through a `def`-style chain of class templates and a lambda per
member, inside one registration function. The numbers are `-c` at `-O2`,
wall time, single runs.

| K | kiln `-c` | `opt` | `llc` | g++ 16.1 `-c` | clang 19 `-c` |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| 1 | 4.47 s | 2.88 s | 1.09 s | 2.27 s | 4.62 s |
| 2 | 20.65 s | 10.99 s | 8.83 s | 6.83 s | 23.74 s |
| 4 | 121.48 s | 45.18 s | 74.04 s | 30.83 s | not run |

Growth exponents:

- kiln `-c`: 2.21 (K 1→2) and 2.56 (K 2→4);
- `opt`: about 2.0;
- `llc`: about 3.0;
- g++: 1.59 and 2.17.

Kiln at K = 2 is at parity with clang 19. Kiln's frontend share stays
small: the syntax pass takes 0.36 / 0.45 / 0.72 s and `-emit-llvm` takes
0.49 / 0.83 / 2.25 s.

At this size the reproducer stresses a different pair of passes from the
real TU:

- at K = 2, DSE is 76% of `opt`;
- in `llc`, the machine scheduler is 45%.

What it shares with the real TU is the term that matters: one function
whose cost to LLVM grows as N^2 to N^3.

### What was fixed (frontend)

**1. Trial folds of non-POD automatic aggregates (`kiln_codegen` `fold.rs`
`Collect::var`).** The fold pre-pass trial-evaluated the initializer of
every automatic array or record. Codegen uses the result only for a POD or
`const` aggregate (clang `IsConstantAggregate`). Each binding's call thunk
holds the whole class by value in a `std::tuple` local, so every thunk
built the N-member object in the Oven. Each member store did a linear
subobject scan, so the total was O(N^3). Such variables are no longer
folded, and the IR does not change.

The second reproducer is `-emit-llvm` only, with IR passes disabled, at
100·K members:

| K | before | after | `-fsyntax-only` |
|---|---|---|---|
| 1 | 0.59 s | 0.48 s | 0.44 s |
| 2 | 1.04 s | 0.72 s | 0.59 s |
| 4 | 2.65 s | 1.25 s | 0.88 s |
| 8 | 11.25 s | 2.68 s | 1.71 s |
| 16 | 70.04 s, 371 MB | 6.45 s, 322 MB | 3.83 s |

From K = 8 to 16, the growth exponent drops from 2.64 to 1.27. Clang 19
takes 6.5 s at K = 16. The bench entry is
`bench/frontend/accessor-wide-emit.cpp`, recorded at 1.96 s and 137 MB
(3.26 s before; clang 19 takes 3.61 s). On the real TU the class is not
wide enough for this to show beyond noise.

**2. Driver peak memory (`kiln_driver` `codegen_cmd.rs` `lower`,
`execute`).** The driver now prints the module after the AST and the Oven
are dropped. The first `opt` run takes the IR text by move rather than by
copy. The real TU's `-emit-llvm` peak falls from 1,044,128 KB to
876,556 KB. The `-c` peak is `llc`'s, at 1.08 GB, and does not change.

### What remains, and why

The brief's target was `-c` at most 115 s. WP-633 did not meet it (WP-641
did: see "WP-641: shrinking the registration function"). About 83% of the
`-c` wall time (92% of `opt`'s pass time) is two LLVM pass runs on one
function. Clang 19 spends more time than kiln on the same TU, and LLVM 22 is slower. The pass pipeline is
unchanged, because no measured pipeline change closes the gap without
dropping passes that every other TU needs. Peak RSS is 1.08 GB, well
under GCC's 2.43 GB.

Follow-ups:

| ID | Title | Where | Evidence | Expected win | Risk | Size |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| F20 | **Done (WP-641): outlining.** Keep the registration function small: emit each binding full-expression's EH cleanups so that landing pads and temporaries' allocas are shared or scoped (`lifetime.start`/`end` per full-expression), or outline very large straight-line bodies | kiln_codegen `outline.rs` (`Cg::emit_stmt_list`, called by `stmt.rs` `emit_compound_without_scope`) | JumpThreading and MemCpyOpt are 92% of `opt`, on one function with 6,455 landing pads and 10,762 allocas. Phase 1 below: landing-pad sharing and lifetime scoping change neither pass; only outlining removes both | Measured: `-c` from about 265 s to about 37 s, RSS unchanged ("WP-641: shrinking the registration function") | High: must stay identical in behaviour, EH order and ABI | L |
| F21 | Measured per-function guard in the driver pipeline | kiln_driver `codegen_cmd.rs:617` `ir_passes` | The knob run above gave no win; a guard would have to skip or bound JumpThreading/MemCpyOpt on functions above a size threshold, measured against code quality | Bounded by the JumpThreading share | Medium: diverges from clang's `-O2` | S |
| F22 | Report the super-linearity upstream. **Issue text written (WP-642), not filed:** `docs/status/upstream/llvm-jumpthreading-memcpyopt.md` | LLVM `JumpThreading`, `MemCpyOpt`, LICM in LLVM 22 | Both clang and kiln IR hit it. A reduced synthetic reproducer is the reproducer above. Clang 19's own IR for `bench/frontend/registration-wide-obj.cpp` reproduces the JumpThreading term (3.8 / 18.8 / 117.9 s at 1,000 / 2,000 / 4,000 bindings), not the MemCpyOpt one. | Long term | None | S |
| F23 | **Done (WP-642): `Types::near` offset index.** Sublinear subobject lookups in the Oven | kiln_oven `ty.rs:1074` `has_subobject`, `ty.rs:308` `field_at`, `ty.rs:1102` `has_bit_unit`, `vm.rs:2475` `union_path` | Linear field scans per store. A constexpr struct whose members hold a union goes from 0.08 s to 0.25 s when its members double from 1,024 to 2,048, which is quadratic. Fields are offset-sorted, so `partition_point` applies. Measured: `bench/oven/wide-struct-store.cpp` 3.45 s to 0.55 s at 16,384 members, exponent 1.75 to 1.00 ("WP-642: sublinear subobject lookups"). Remaining linear scans: `notes.rs:1371` `union_field_at` (diagnostic notes only) and the `empty_bases` scan at `ty.rs:1246` in `has_subobject`. | Removes the quadratic term for wide constexpr classes | Low | S–M |

F11 above (cache subobject and union paths) overlaps F23. F23 is the
narrower form, which needs no cache.

## WP-641: shrinking the registration function

F20 above. The production TU's `-O2 -c` drops from about 260 s to about
40 s. The fix is that kiln outlines runs of statements out of any function
with at least 256 call-containing expression statements, into internal
`noinline` helpers (spec/landed/codegen/WP-641.md).

### Phase 1: IR surgery

Each variant is the same TU's unoptimised IR (`-c -emit-llvm -Xclang
-disable-llvm-passes`), changed in one way and timed with `opt-19
-passes='default<O2>' -time-passes`. The IR counts are for the largest
function before `opt`, counted by this WP's script (the instruction count
includes the lifetime calls, so it differs from WP-633's). "opt" is the
whole module's `opt` wall time. Runs were sequential, one at a time.

Production TU:

| Variant | Instructions | Blocks | Landing pads | Allocas | opt | JumpThreading | MemCpyOpt |
|---|---:|---:|---:|---:|---:|---:|---:|
| Baseline (two runs) | 107,615 | 17,221 | 6,455 | 10,762 | 252 / 266 s | 144 / 146 s | 68 / 81 s |
| No exceptions: every invoke a call, the pads dead (IR surgery; `-fno-exceptions` rejects the TU's `throw`) | 107,615 | 17,221 | 6,455, all dead | 10,762 | 125 s | 0.4 s | 86 s |
| No lifetime markers (the only EH cleanup, so one pad is left) | 28,012 | 8,616 | 1 | 10,762 | 241 s | 131 s | 75 s |
| `lifetime.end` on the normal path only | 49,528 | 8,616 | 1 | 10,762 | 251 s | 144 s | 68 s |
| Each statement's invokes unwind to one shared pad (2,150 edges redirected) | 107,615 | 17,221 | 6,455 | 10,762 | 275 s | 151 s | 82 s |
| Statements outlined (this WP) | 366 | 52 | 4 | 30 | 37 s | 0.3 s | 0.2 s |

Kiln already brackets every full-expression temporary with
`lifetime.start`/`end` at `-O2`: 32,274 markers. So variant 3 of the brief
(tight lifetimes) is the baseline, and the two lifetime rows test the
opposite direction. The two costs have different causes:

- **JumpThreading.** The invokes drive it. Its block merging moves an
  accumulated block, headed by the entry block's allocas, once per merge
  along the inlined chain.
- **MemCpyOpt.** The runs of field stores into 10,762 allocas drive it.
  `tryMergingIntoMemset` updates one MemorySSA spanning the whole
  function. It does not depend on the exception edges.

Neither landing-pad sharing nor lifetime scoping changes either cost.
Removing exceptions entirely halves `opt` and still misses the target.
Only splitting the function removes both costs.

Reproducer `bench/frontend/registration-wide-obj.cpp` (4,000 bindings in one
function):

| Variant | Instructions | Blocks | Landing pads | Allocas | opt | JumpThreading | MemCpyOpt |
|---|---:|---:|---:|---:|---:|---:|---:|
| Baseline (two runs) | 72,010 | 8,002 | 4,000 | 8,003 | 116 / 91 s | 110 / 85 s | 0.06 s |
| `-fno-exceptions` | 32,003 | 1 | 0 | 8,001 | 110 s | 104 s | 0.06 s |
| Invokes made calls (IR surgery) | 72,010 | 8,002 | 4,000, all dead | 8,003 | 111 s | 104 s | 0.07 s |
| No lifetime markers | 60,010 | 8,002 | 4,000 | 8,003 | 86 s | 80 s | 0.06 s |
| `lifetime.end` on the normal path only | 68,010 | 8,002 | 4,000 | 8,003 | 90 s | 84 s | 0.06 s |
| Shared pads (one invoke per statement: nothing to share) | 72,010 | 8,002 | 4,000 | 8,003 | 99 s | 93 s | 0.06 s |
| Statements outlined (this WP; largest helper) | 584 | 66 | 32 | 66 | 6.2 s | 0.2 s | 0.05 s |

The reproducer reproduces the JumpThreading term only. Its chain comes
from the inlined `def` body's folded branches, so it survives
`-fno-exceptions`. It does not reproduce the MemCpyOpt term, which needs
the production descriptors' longer runs of field stores. Outlining is 15x to 19x on
it.

Threshold sweep, the same shape at N bindings, `opt` inline vs outlined:
128 is 0.17 s (below the threshold), 256 is 0.39 vs 0.34 s, 512 is 1.09
vs 0.66 s, 1,024 is 3.45 vs 1.35 s. Below a few hundred statements the
difference is noise, so the threshold is 256, and below it the IR is
byte-identical.

### Production TU A/B (`prod633.sh`, `-O2 -c`, back to back)

| Pair | main (harness build) | WP-641 (harness build) |
|---|---:|---:|
| 1 | 267.3 s, 1,081,164 KB | 36.7 s, 1,053,116 KB |
| 2 | 265.1 s, 1,079,624 KB | 36.9 s, 1,053,860 KB |

That is 7.2x on the production compile, now under the 115 s target, and
peak RSS is 2.6% lower. Of the remaining 37 s, the frontend up to
unoptimised IR is about 15.7 s (the `-emit-llvm` runs above), and the
rest is `opt` and `llc` over the whole module. The standalone `opt`
times in the phase-1 tables include parsing textual IR and `-time-passes`,
so they do not add up to these times.

## WP-642: sublinear subobject lookups

F23 above. Every by-offset lookup into a class now goes through
`Types::near` (`crates/kiln_oven/src/ty.rs`), an offset index built once
per record with at least 16 fields, so a store into a wide class no
longer scans its members (spec/oven.md "Offset index",
spec/landed/oven/WP-642.md).

### The shapes

- `bench/oven/wide-struct-store.cpp`: a constexpr struct of N members,
  each holding a two-member union. Every member is stored through each
  union member in turn (16 rounds), then read back. Each store walks the
  union path to the member, which was a linear scan per store.
- `bench/oven/wide-struct-read.cpp`: N members without a union, each
  stored once and then read 1,024 times. No union activity.

N is `1 << WIDE_LOG2`, so k = 12 is 4,096 members, the recorded size.
The numbers are `-fsyntax-only -fconstexpr-steps=900000000`, wall time
and peak RSS from `/usr/bin/time`, two back-to-back main/branch pairs
per size, one process at a time (main is d642a3689).

| shape | k (N) | main pair 1 | branch pair 1 | main pair 2 | branch pair 2 |
|---|---|---:|---:|---:|---:|
| store | 12 (4,096) | 0.35 s, 31,796 KB | 0.16 s, 33,580 KB | 0.32 s, 31,820 KB | 0.14 s, 31,736 KB |
| store | 13 (8,192) | 1.02 s, 52,784 KB | 0.28 s, 52,656 KB | 1.03 s, 52,788 KB | 0.27 s, 50,824 KB |
| store | 14 (16,384) | 3.45 s, 90,636 KB | 0.55 s, 89,920 KB | 3.44 s, 91,820 KB | 0.55 s, 92,288 KB |
| read | 12 (4,096) | 0.33 s, 30,088 KB | 0.33 s, 29,956 KB | 0.34 s, 30,084 KB | 0.33 s, 29,812 KB |
| read | 13 (8,192) | 0.68 s, 47,444 KB | 0.65 s, 47,400 KB | 0.70 s, 48,072 KB | 0.64 s, 47,392 KB |
| read | 14 (16,384) | 1.48 s, 84,376 KB | 1.29 s, 85,692 KB | 1.49 s, 85,656 KB | 1.31 s, 84,656 KB |

Growth exponents per doubling (means of the pairs):

- store, main: 1.61 and 1.75. Branch: 0.87 and 1.00.
- read, main: 1.04 and 1.11. Branch: 0.97 and 1.01.

So the union path was the quadratic one, as F23 said. The read shape was
already close to linear and gains 12% at k = 14. At 16,384 members the
store shape is 6.3x faster. Peak RSS does not change: the index is 12
bytes per field of an indexed record plus one map entry per member,
against about 5 KB of RSS per member that both binaries spend.

Two terms had to go. The first was the per-store scans (`field_at`,
`member_toward`, `extent_in`, `has_subobject`, `has_bit_unit`, and the
`union_path`/`mutable_path`/`place_in_unions` fallbacks in
`crates/kiln_oven/src/vm.rs`). After that, callgrind on the store shape
at k = 13 put about 15% in the compiler's member lookup:
`Compiler::member` in `compile.rs` found a member's field index by a
linear `position`, once per member access expression, so a body naming
each of N members was quadratic to compile. `Types::field_index` makes
it a map lookup.

The recorded baselines (`cargo xtask bench --record --filter wide-struct
bench/oven`, five runs) are:

- `oven/wide-struct-store`: 154.2 ms, 30.9 MB;
- `oven/wide-struct-read`: 345.4 ms, 29.3 MB.

### S7 and S8

The runs used `cargo xtask bench --compiler <bin> bench/reflect/s7.cpp
bench/reflect/s8.cpp`, which takes the medians of five runs. Main and
branch alternated, three pairs, one process at a time:

| pair | S7 main | S7 branch | S8 main | S8 branch |
|---|---:|---:|---:|---:|
| 1 | 545.1 ms, 49.2 MB | 553.5 ms, 48.9 MB | 710.0 ms, 75.8 MB | 688.3 ms, 77.3 MB |
| 2 | 544.1 ms, 49.2 MB | 550.6 ms, 48.9 MB | 695.0 ms, 76.1 MB | 696.1 ms, 76.3 MB |
| 3 | 551.0 ms, 49.3 MB | 549.7 ms, 48.9 MB | 691.6 ms, 75.9 MB | 699.4 ms, 76.4 MB |

Neither moves: S7 is +0.8% and S8 is -0.4%, both within the spread, and
the Oven step counts are identical. The S7 lookups that F11 counts are
into small library classes (string, vector and their bases), below the
16-field index threshold, where `near` is the scan it replaces. The
lookups are many short ones, not one long one. F11's cost is the number of
path walks, which only a cache would cut. F11 therefore stays open, and
F23 does not touch it.

## WP-644: subobject path cache

F11 above. The walks from an allocation's root to the object an access
designates (`union_path`, `mutable_path`, `place_in_unions` and
`extent_of` in `crates/kiln_oven/src/vm.rs`) now take their
memory-independent steps from a cache in `Types`
(`crates/kiln_oven/src/ty/path.rs`, spec/oven.md "Path cache"). Only the
active-member reads stay per access.

### Walk counts (main, temporary counters)

| program | `union_path` | `place_in_unions` | `extent_of` | `has_subobject` (with recursion) | `member_toward` |
|---|---:|---:|---:|---:|---:|
| S7 (N = 40) | 794,167 calls, 1,708,436 levels (2.15 each), 21 distinct keys; 233,321 union levels | 39,042 calls, 4.00 levels each, 16 keys | 192,677 calls, 3.00 levels each, 6 keys | 2,127,541 calls, 7,989 keys | 503,731 calls, 22 keys |
| S7 (N = 8) | 161,347 calls, 21 keys; 47,329 union levels | 7,888 | 39,057 | 431,539, 4,190 keys | 102,271 |
| wide-struct-store (k = 12) | 69,632 calls, 4.00 levels each, 8,192 keys | none | none | 491,520 | 139,264, 8,194 keys |

S7 never calls `mutable_path`. S8 walks only in `extent_of`: 31,469
calls, 2.00 levels each, 19 keys. A key is (root type, offset, target
type).

Callgrind on S7 at N = 8 (full program; the run loop is recursive, so the
self cost of the walk functions is summed): `has_subobject`,
`union_path`, `field_at`, `extent_in`, `extent_of`, `near`, `slow_access`,
`same_unqualified` and `member_toward` take 96.8 M of 3,409.9 M Ir
(2.84%), about a fifth of the Oven's 14.1%. At N = 16 they take 4.5%, and
each added class costs 11.8 M Ir of walks out of 100.9 M Ir, 11.7% of
it. That extrapolates to about 7% at N = 40 and 9.3% at N = 100, the 9%
F11 quotes.

### The cache

`Types::hop(walk, t, off, p)` is the walk from record `t` toward the
object of type `p` at byte `off`, up to its first array or union. At a
union it returns a mask of the members that may hold the object, so the
walk reads the active and possibly-active members from memory and tests
mask bits. `Types::extent` and `has_subobject` cache their answer for
the first record below any leading arrays. Each of the three maps holds
at most `PATH_CACHE_MAX` = 16,384 entries and is cleared by the insert
past that.

Hit rates (temporary counters, all three maps together):

| program | hits | misses | clears | entries at exit | footprint |
|---|---:|---:|---:|---:|---:|
| S7 (N = 40) | 654,396 | 58 | 0 | 58 | 3,731 bytes |
| S8 | 31,450 | 19 | 0 | 19 | 1,148 bytes |
| wide-struct-store k = 12 | 61,440 | 8,192 | 0 | 8,192 | 0.70 MB |
| wide-struct-store k = 13 | 122,880 | 16,384 | 0 | 16,384 | 1.40 MB |
| wide-struct-store k = 14 | 16,384 | 262,144 | 15 | 16,384 | 1.40 MB |

The store shape has one key per member, so at k = 14 its 16,384 members
plus the other paths exceed the cap and the hop map thrashes. It is
still no slower, because a miss costs the walk it replaces plus one
insert.

Callgrind on S7 at N = 8 with the cache: 3,351.2 M Ir in total (-1.7%).
The walk functions, plus `Types::hop`, take about 52 M Ir, down from
96.8 M.

### S7 and S8

`cargo xtask bench --compiler <bin> bench/reflect/s7.cpp
bench/reflect/s8.cpp`, medians of five runs, main (811a810fe) and branch
alternating, one process at a time:

| pair | S7 main | S7 branch | S8 main | S8 branch |
|---|---:|---:|---:|---:|
| 1 | 550.9 ms, 49.4 MB | 539.0 ms, 51.1 MB | 705.3 ms, 77.3 MB | 716.7 ms, 76.5 MB |
| 2 | 558.4 ms, 49.3 MB | 540.4 ms, 49.3 MB | 705.1 ms, 76.8 MB | 710.0 ms, 75.7 MB |
| 3 | 551.4 ms, 49.3 MB | 538.9 ms, 51.2 MB | 698.9 ms, 76.0 MB | 699.2 ms, 76.7 MB |

S7 is 2.6% faster (means 553.6 and 539.4 ms), outside the 0.5–2.3%
spread. S8 does not move (+0.8%, within its spread): it only walks in
`extent_of`. The Oven step counts are identical.

Peak RSS on S7 is bimodal in both binaries: the 51.1 and 51.2 MB pairs
are the upper mode, not the cache, which holds 3.7 KB. Twelve more
alternating runs each under `/usr/bin/time`:

| | wall median | RSS median | RSS min, max | runs in the upper mode |
|---|---:|---:|---:|---:|
| S7 main | 0.540 s | 47,026 KB | 46,948, 49,056 KB | 3 of 12 |
| S7 branch | 0.520 s | 46,980 KB | 46,840, 48,872 KB | 2 of 12 |
| S8 main | 0.660 s | 66,464 KB | 64,764, 68,120 KB | 10 of 12 |
| S8 branch | 0.665 s | 66,044 KB | 64,712, 67,048 KB | 8 of 12 |

So peak RSS does not rise: -0.1% on S7 and -0.6% on S8.

### The wide shapes

As in "WP-642: sublinear subobject lookups": `-fsyntax-only
-fconstexpr-steps=900000000`, `/usr/bin/time`, two main/branch pairs per
size, one process at a time.

| shape | k (N) | main pair 1 | branch pair 1 | main pair 2 | branch pair 2 |
|---|---|---:|---:|---:|---:|
| store | 12 (4,096) | 0.14 s, 31,940 KB | 0.13 s, 33,240 KB | 0.14 s, 31,932 KB | 0.13 s, 33,224 KB |
| store | 13 (8,192) | 0.27 s, 53,500 KB | 0.26 s, 53,996 KB | 0.28 s, 53,500 KB | 0.28 s, 54,152 KB |
| store | 14 (16,384) | 0.55 s, 91,668 KB | 0.55 s, 93,940 KB | 0.54 s, 91,468 KB | 0.55 s, 93,792 KB |
| read | 12 (4,096) | 0.33 s, 30,136 KB | 0.33 s, 32,252 KB | 0.34 s, 32,240 KB | 0.33 s, 30,136 KB |
| read | 13 (8,192) | 0.66 s, 49,832 KB | 0.67 s, 47,956 KB | 0.66 s, 49,808 KB | 0.67 s, 50,744 KB |
| read | 14 (16,384) | 1.35 s, 86,584 KB | 1.33 s, 86,676 KB | 1.34 s, 87,352 KB | 1.32 s, 85,444 KB |

Time does not change. The store shape's RSS rises by 1.3, 0.6 and 2.3 MB
(4.1%, 1.0% and 2.5%), the hop map's one entry per member. The cap bounds
it at about 1.4 MB per map. The read shape has no union and does not
touch the cache; its RSS is bimodal in both binaries.

The baselines were not re-recorded. The S7 win is real but small, and
the recorded S7 baseline (534.0 ms, recorded at a lower host load) is
below what both binaries measure now, so a re-record would raise it.

### What remains

- The cached walk still costs two hash lookups per union level
  (`Memory::unions` and `union_alts`, `vm.rs:2488` `union_path`) and one
  per record level (`ty/path.rs:144` `Types::hop`).
- A record with more distinct (offset, type) paths than the cap (a class
  of more than 16,384 members, or one holding a large array of unions)
  thrashes the hop map. It is no slower than without the cache. Keying
  the hops of a record's array member by member, not by byte
  (`ty/path.rs` `Types::hop_in`), would bound the keys by the member
  count.

## WP-645: allocation churn

F5, F6 and F12 above (spec/landed/sema/WP-645.md). The four in-tree
shapes were run under `valgrind --tool=dhat` (allocation sites, with
`ulimit -v 3000000`, `timeout 1200`) and `--tool=callgrind` (`malloc`
call counts and the `memcpy` edges). DHAT is used for the sites:
callgrind's call graph is unreliable on this host ("Inputs and method"),
and DHAT records the full stack of every allocation. The commands are
spec/bench.md's (`-fsyntax-only`; `accessor-wide-emit` at `-S
-emit-llvm`, so the codegen half is in). Main is 811a810fe.

### Attribution on main

Allocation calls (callgrind, `malloc` + `realloc` + `calloc`) and heap
blocks (DHAT):

| shape | Ir | allocation calls | DHAT blocks |
|---|--:|--:|--:|
| `templates_wide` | 3.46 G | 2,785,946 | 2,846,794 |
| `templates_deep` | 0.73 G | 103,194 | 103,973 |
| S8 (N=100) | 6.59 G | 7,418,459 | 7,502,355 |
| `accessor-wide-emit` | 11.43 G | 13,376,681 | 13,570,984 |

The top sites by blocks, with the allocations made inside the
`ExprArena::children` worklist helper charged to the function that
called it (so `begin_loc` is the `Vec` its child loop built). Kiln
frames only; the percentage is of the shape's blocks:

| site | function | `templates_wide` | `templates_deep` | S8 | `accessor-wide-emit` |
|---|---|--:|--:|--:|--:|
| `kiln_ast/src/expr.rs:2659` | `Ast::begin_loc` (children `Vec` per node) | 30,007 (1.1%) | 1 | 75,350 (1.0%) | 2,983,214 (22.0%) |
| `kiln_sema/src/lookup.rs:181`, `:182` | `Sema::filter` (`Found` pushes) | 4,944 (0.2%) | 24,036 (23.1%) | 636,550 (8.5%) | 607,906 (4.5%) |

| `kiln_sema/src/conv/arith.rs:49` | `common_sugar` (both desugaring chains) | 160,012 (5.6%) | – | 8,481 (0.1%) | 9,585 (0.1%) |
| `kiln_sema/src/class/special/implicit.rs:317` | `special_fields` | 123,648 (4.3%) | 4,519 (4.3%) | 4,351 (0.1%) | – |

| `kiln_sema/src/overload/redecl.rs:148`, `:149` | `heads_equivalent` (both parameter lists) | – | – | 329,798 (4.4%) | – |
| `kiln_ast/src/types/args.rs:223` | `TypeContext::arg_list` (canonical copy) | 60,503 (2.1%) | 1,999 (1.9%) | 88,076 (1.2%) | 32,774 (0.2%) |
| `kiln_sema/src/class/access.rs:106` | `effective_context_at` | 60,300 (2.1%) | 2,997 (2.9%) | 9,519 (0.1%) | 6,560 |
| `kiln_abi/src/layout/mod.rs:292` | record layout `to_vec` | 60,000 (2.1%) | 1,000 (1.0%) | 122,110 (1.6%) | 121,263 (0.9%) |
| `kiln_sema/src/lookup.rs:996` | `current_scope_raw_impl` | 50,209 (1.8%) | 2,005 (1.9%) | – | – |

F12's node meter (`Ast::node_live_bytes`) made 200, 2, 9,682 and 13,056
blocks on these shapes; it matters on the 200-class production compile

**`with_oven`'s `memcpy`.** `with_oven` is inlined into its callers, so
the move shows as `memcpy` calls from `eval_const` and `try_fold`, about
430 Ir each: 0.61% of S8's Ir (45,514 + 42,231 calls) and 1.37% of

### Fixes, one commit each

Before is the site's count on main, after is the same site on the
branch (DHAT blocks, the shape named):

| commit | fix | shape | before | after |
|---|---|---|--:|--:|
| b9605c276 | F6: `Sema.oven: Option<Box<Oven>>` (Ir, not allocations; F6 below) | `templates_wide` / S8 | 3.460 G / 6.590 G Ir | 3.311 G / 6.454 G Ir |
| bd12f0d89 | F12: the node meter sums `VecDims` / `map_live_bytes` in the annotation, attribute, redeclaration and lookup arenas | S8 / `accessor-wide-emit` | 9,682 / 13,056 | 0 / 0 |
| ddd196823 | `ExprArena::children_each` / `first_child`: worklists append in place, first-child loops build nothing | `accessor-wide-emit` (`begin_loc`) | 2,983,214 | 0 |
| 12ddd9f51 | one Sema scratch stack for the six full-expression walks | `templates_wide` | 1,010,619 | 1 |
| 7587fef27 | `common_sugar` walks the chains in place | `templates_wide` | 160,012 | 0 |
| d385208a1, c8a890f0e | `Sema::filter` takes an iterator; namespace scope chains its own results with the nominated ones | `templates_deep` (`lookup.rs:480`/`:488`) | 12,006 | 0 |
| 9e281ed22 | the declarator name is spelled only for a diagnostic | `templates_wide` (`sym_str`) | 100,412 | 40,203 |
| 2ed6eaded | `heads_equivalent` zips; constraint profiles go to a reused `EquivScratch` | S8 | 755,483 | 0 |
| 037927d60 | `special_fields_iter` for the callers that only filter | `templates_wide` | 123,648 | 82,434 |
| e49735b38 | `arg_list` copies to canonical only when an argument is not canonical | `templates_wide` | 60,503 | 0 |

Totals, main → branch (DHAT blocks):

| shape | main | branch | change |
|---|--:|--:|--:|
| `templates_wide` | 2,846,794 | 1,493,008 | −47.6% |
| `templates_deep` | 103,973 | 86,692 | −16.6% |

### F6: the boxed Oven

Boxing removed four `memcpy` edges, not two. Besides `with_oven`'s take
and put-back (inlined into `eval_const` and `try_fold`), `Oven::evaluate`
and `Oven::eval_job` also copied the `Oven` by value, at the same 430 Ir
per call. On main the four were 1.50% + 1.25% + 0.75% + 0.62% of
`templates_wide` (142.5 M Ir) and 0.59% + 0.55% + 0.30% + 0.28% of S8
(113.2 M Ir). None remains in the F6-only profile, and they account for
most of its Ir drop: 148.8 M on `templates_wide`, 135.9 M on S8.

The targets: allocations are −46% on `templates_wide` (the attributed
shape), −22% on S8 and −30% on `accessor-wide-emit` (target −25%); wall
is −16.7% on `templates_wide` and −6.1% on S8 (target −3% on two
shapes). No shape is slower. The `-c -O2` shapes move by less than their
spread, because `opt` and `llc` dominate them (`accessor-wide-emit`
spends about 110 s of its 119 s there). RSS moves by at most +0.5%
(`templates_wide`, 336 KB), within the run-to-run spread and under the
1% bound. `templates_deep` runs for 50 ms, below the timer's 10 ms
resolution for a −4% change.

`-fkiln-instantiation-report` (`=all`, `=json`, `=text`) on
`templates_wide`, `templates_deep` and S8 is byte-identical between main
and the branch except for the millisecond and `self_us`/`incl_us`
fields.

**Baselines.** `templates_wide` and S8 moved by 5% or more, so both were
re-recorded (`cargo xtask bench --record --filter frontend/templates_wide
bench/frontend`, and `--runs 3 --filter reflect/s8 bench/reflect` as
WP-640 recorded it): 292.98 ms and 664.30 ms. The run flagged
`templates_wide`'s memory (68.4 → 77.3 MB) against its 2026-11-03
baseline. That drift predates this package: main itself measures 77.5
and 77.0 MB, against 77.2 and 76.8 MB for the branch, under the same
harness back to back.

### What remains, and why

Each remaining site is under 2% of its shape's allocations, or needs a
redesign that is out of this package's scope:

- `crates/kiln_sema/src/lookup.rs:181-182` `Sema::filter`: the
  `Found` result holds two `Vec`s (decls, found), so every non-empty
  lookup makes two allocations (S8 10.9%, `templates_deep` 27.7%,
  `accessor-wide-emit` 6.4% of the branch's blocks). Single-result
  inline storage changes `Found`'s type everywhere it is used: a
  redesign.
- `crates/kiln_sema/src/class/special/implicit.rs:313`
  `special_fields`: the remaining callers keep the `Vec` across calls
  that need `&mut self` (`class/special/deletion.rs:432`); 5.5% of
  `templates_wide` on the branch. Caching the field list per record is
  the fix.
- `crates/kiln_sema/src/class/access.rs:106` `effective_context_at`
  (4.0% of `templates_wide`), `crates/kiln_sema/src/lookup.rs:996`
  `current_scope_raw_impl` (3.4%): both return a fresh scope chain per
  query; an iterator over the scope stack needs the stack's borrow
  across the caller's lookups.
- `crates/kiln_abi/src/layout/mod.rs:292`, `layout/props.rs:148`,
  `layout/record.rs:133`: per-record layout vectors, built once per
  record (4.0% + 2.0% + 2.0% of `templates_wide`). They are the layout's
  own storage, not churn.
- `crates/kiln_sema/src/decl.rs:4653` `act_on_field` → `decl.rs:130`
  `sym_str`: the field's name `String` (2.7% of `templates_wide`); the record field
  table stores it.
- `crates/kiln_codegen/src/fir.rs` `Op::operands` (2.2% of
  `accessor-wide-emit`) and `crates/kiln_abi/src/mangle.rs:721`
  `decl_name` (2.1%): an operand slice view and a mangler that writes
  into its output; both change a public signature used across codegen.
- Overload resolution: `crates/kiln_sema/src/templ/funcs.rs`
  `proto_param_types`, `templ/deduce_call.rs:323` `deduce_spec`,
  `overload/call.rs:294`/`:297` `add_template_candidate`,
  `templ/subst/mod.rs:367-368` `Substituter::args`: 1–2% each on S8 and
  `accessor-wide-emit`. They belong with F13 (fewer attempts) rather
  than with buffer reuse.
- `crates/kiln_oven/src/compile/builtins.rs:127` and
  `crates/kiln_codegen/src/fold.rs:697` still call the allocating
  `children()`; neither is above 0.5% on these shapes.

## WP-648: interpreter dispatch

F9 above (spec/landed/oven/WP-648.md). Main is 8b6f177ea.

### Inputs and method

The Ir counts are from `valgrind --tool=cachegrind --cache-sim=no`
(under `ulimit -v 3000000` and `timeout 1200`, with
`-fkiln-stack-size=256m` so the 1 GiB default stack reservation fits).
The shapes are the four in-tree Oven bench entries that execute more
than a few hundred instructions, `virtual-dispatch` and S7, each with
its spec/bench.md command line. callgrind did not work here, for two
reasons. `--toggle-collect='*run_loop*'` turns collection off at the
first nested `run_loop` (a bridge callback that evaluates), and
callgrind ran out of memory under the 3 GB limit on `table-nohdr` and
`churn-vector`.

"`run_loop` self" is the Ir of every line inlined into the `run_loop`
function: `vm.rs` plus the `kbc.rs`, `index.rs` and `Vec` lines that
are inlined into it. Callees that are not inlined (`store`, `ptr_add`,
`enter`, `mem`) are excluded. The executed-instruction counts come
from the new `op-stats` feature (`cargo build --release --features
kiln_oven/op-stats`). At exit it prints the dynamic opcode histogram
and the adjacent-pair histogram to stderr, and it compiles to nothing
without the feature.

| shape | `run_loop` self Ir | program Ir | share | instructions | Ir / instruction |
|---|--:|--:|--:|--:|--:|
| `sieve-nohdr` | 114,738,821 | 171,244,215 | 67.0% | 2,103,515 | 54.5 |
| `sort-nohdr` | 300,273,562 | 459,980,906 | 65.3% | 4,014,952 | 74.8 |
| `table-nohdr` | 691,653,149 | 907,870,319 | 76.2% | 12,581,848 | 55.0 |
| `virtual-dispatch` | 192,023,445 | 279,187,395 | 68.8% | 2,767,152 | 69.4 |
| `churn-vector` | 8,977,534,428 | 14,851,167,729 | 60.5% | 134,251,174 | 66.9 |
| S7 | 1,117,720,263 | 6,254,016,462 | 17.9% | 16,736,927 | 66.8 |

`fib` executes 417 instructions (its calls are memoized) and S8 executes
736,100, so dispatch does not matter for either.

**Where an instruction's Ir goes** (`run_loop` self Ir per executed
instruction, split by source line). "Fetch" is `self.code[pc]`,
`from_u8` and the `match op` jump. "Operands" is the `arg!`/`reg!` index
arithmetic. "Bounds" is `index.rs`, the slice bounds checks of those
indexings. "Vec" is the `Vec` pointer and length reloads (`alloc` `vec`
`mod.rs`). "Unattributed" is line 0, which is almost all spills and
reloads of `pc` and `fb`: the disassembly has 236 stack loads and
stores of the two. The rest is the arm bodies.

| shape | Ir / insn | fetch | operands | bounds | Vec | unattributed | arm bodies |
|---|--:|--:|--:|--:|--:|--:|--:|
| `sieve-nohdr` | 54.5 | 13.1 (24%) | 7.0 (13%) | 12.0 (22%) | 5.0 (9%) | 6.9 (13%) | 10.5 (19%) |
| `sort-nohdr` | 74.8 | 13.2 (18%) | 7.1 (10%) | 14.2 (19%) | 6.7 (9%) | 8.8 (12%) | 24.8 (33%) |
| `table-nohdr` | 55.0 | 13.0 (24%) | 7.0 (13%) | 12.6 (23%) | 5.3 (10%) | 6.9 (12%) | 10.1 (18%) |
| `virtual-dispatch` | 69.4 | 13.0 (19%) | 6.8 (10%) | 14.1 (20%) | 7.2 (10%) | 8.8 (13%) | 19.5 (28%) |
| `churn-vector` | 66.9 | 13.2 (20%) | 7.8 (12%) | 14.4 (21%) | 5.9 (9%) | 7.9 (12%) | 17.7 (26%) |
| S7 | 66.8 | 13.2 (20%) | 6.2 (9%) | 12.8 (19%) | 7.3 (11%) | 7.8 (12%) | 19.5 (29%) |

So about 70% of an instruction's cost is dispatch overhead rather than
the work it does. `run_loop` is 51.7 KB of machine code. `load` is
inlined into it, while `store` and `ptr_add` are not. With that many
arms, `pc`, `fb`, the op byte and the `code`/`regs` base pointers do not
stay in registers: every `arg!` reloads `self.code`'s pointer and
length, checks the index and loads the word, and `reg!` repeats the same
steps for `self.regs`.

**Opcode histogram** (share of executed instructions, top entries):

|---|--:|--:|--:|--:|--:|--:|
| `Mov` | 27.4 | 13.3 | 21.6 | 26.7 | 18.8 | 20.4 |
| `Const` | 7.1 | 12.8 | 20.4 | 6.8 | 15.6 | 7.7 |
| `Step` | 9.4 | 14.4 | 5.9 | 7.7 | 6.2 | 13.8 |
| `JmpIfNot` | 9.4 | 7.1 | 8.7 | 1.8 | 6.2 | 5.2 |
