**Adjacent pairs** (share of executed instructions):

| pair | sieve | sort-nohdr | table-nohdr | virtual-dispatch | churn-vector | S7 |
|---|--:|--:|--:|--:|--:|--:|
| `Mov+Mov` | 13.3 | | 5.6 | 8.6 | 3.1 | 2.5 |
| `JmpIfNot+Step` | 9.2 | 4.8 | 5.5 | | 6.2 | 4.9 |
| `ICmp+JmpIfNot` | 7.1 | 7.1 | 5.6 | | 6.2 | 3.0 |
| `Mov+Const` | | | 9.2 | 3.6 | 9.4 | 2.2 |
| `IAdd+Mov` | 7.1 | | 5.6 | 3.6 | 6.2 | |
| `Const+IAdd` | | 5.5 | 5.1 | | 9.4 | |
| `Mov+Jmp` | 6.8 | | 5.1 | | 6.2 | |
| `Step+Const` | 4.4 | 3.7 | 4.2 | | | 2.9 |
| `PtrAdd+Load` | | 6.7 | | | 6.2 | |
| `PtrAdd+Store` | 4.4 | | | | 6.2 | |
| `Step+Mov` | | 4.1 | | | | 5.6 |
| `Mov+Field` | | | | 4.5 | | 2.9 |

The opcode mix is flat. No pair covers more than about 13% of any
shape, and `Mov` alone covers 13–27%. Fusing pairs can therefore remove
only a few percent of dispatches. The bigger lever is the cost per
dispatch.

### Design note

The target is −25% `run_loop` self Ir on the Oven shapes. For S7, where
`run_loop` is 17.9% of all Ir, −5% wall needs about −28% of `run_loop`.
The candidates, each with the evidence for and against it:

1. **Pre-decoded instructions** (a parallel `Vec<Insn>` of decoded op
   and operands). Against: the pc is an index into `code` for sites,
   step locations, overflow sites, notes (`notes.rs` reads `code[pc+k]`),
   builtin arguments, meta, `exc`, `life` and every frame's `ret_pc`.
   A second instruction space means either rewriting all of those or
   keeping a pc map. A fixed-size `Insn` of the six-operand maximum is 28
   bytes or more against today's average of about 3 words, which is
   roughly ×2.5 bytecode memory. Bytecode is a measurable part of peak
   RSS on S7/S8, so this fails the +2% bound. Rejected.
2. **One fetch window per instruction.** One checked slice read
   `code[pc..pc+8]`, copied into a local `[u32; 8]` that covers the op
   word and the largest operand count (6), so operand words are read
   from the window without a check. This removes most of the "bounds"
   and "Vec" columns for operand words: about 8–10 Ir per instruction,
   15% of `run_loop`. The encoding does not change. Code is padded with
   7 `Nop` words at the end of every compiled unit, so the window always
   fits. When it does not fit (code read mid-compilation by a nested
   evaluation), a cold fallback copies into a zero-padded window. That
   fallback still panics, as `code[pc+k]` does today, if an instruction
   runs past the end. Chosen.
3. **One register-window borrow** (`&mut self.regs[fb..fb+nregs]`).
   Against: every arm that calls a `&mut self` method (`load`, `store`,
   `enter`, every `mem` call) has to end the borrow and take it again,
   and each `reg!` still needs its own bounds check against the
   window's length. That saves the `Vec` reload but not the check: a few
   Ir on `Mov`/`Const` only. Masking the index to make the check
   disappear changes the failure mode of a bad register from a panic to a
   wrong value. Rejected.
4. **Cold arms out of line** (`#[inline(never)]` methods). There are
   about 30 arms of 13 to 43 lines (`Ret`'s frame teardown, `New`,
   `Delete`, `EnumCheck`, `MpFn`, `VCall`, `Placement`, `ZeroInit`,
   `ScopeExit`, `TypeId`, `IsConstEval`, ...). Their slow paths spill
   `pc`/`fb` and raise register pressure across the whole function.
   Moving the bodies of the rarely executed arms, and the fault and
   slow paths of the hot ones, into `#[inline(never)]` methods that take
   `(pc, fb, window, aux)` targets the "unattributed" column and part of
   "fetch" (5–8 Ir). Its effect on LLVM's register allocation cannot be
   predicted, so this step is measured on its own. Chosen as the second
   step.
5. **Superinstructions.** `ICmp+JmpIfNot` (3–7%) and `JmpIfNot+Step`
   (5–9%) are the only pairs that appear in every loop shape. A fused
   `JmpIfNot+Step` must still report a step fault at the `Step`'s pc and
   keep the counters identical. That is possible by fusing in place: the
   `JmpIfNot` arm checks whether the next word is a `Step` and runs it
   inline, so the old sequence stays the encoding and nothing addresses
   a new pc. The saving is one dispatch on 5–9% of instructions, which
   is 3–6% of `run_loop`. Kept in reserve, used only if steps 2 and 4
   miss the target.
6. **A cheaper `Step` check.** `Step` is 6–14% of instructions, but its
   arm is already one add, one compare and one store of the counter.
   Keeping the counters byte-identical leaves almost nothing to remove
   beyond what the window saves. Not pursued on its own.
7. **Threaded code, computed goto, a JIT, `get_unchecked`.** Excluded by
   the brief and by `forbid(unsafe_code)`.

The plan is three commits, each measured with the same cachegrind runs:
(A) the fetch window and the padding, (B) the cold arms out of line, and
(C) the in-place `JmpIfNot+Step` and `ICmp+JmpIfNot` fusion, only if A
and B leave the Oven shapes short of −25%.

### Per-step results

Each step is one commit on `wp/648`, measured with the cachegrind runs
above. "Dispatch Ir" is `run_loop` self Ir plus, from step B on, the
self Ir of `run_cold` (the out-of-line cold arms), per executed
instruction. The instruction counts are main's, which the steps do not
change (`op-stats` counts a fused instruction as executed).

| shape | main | A: window | B: cold arms | C: fusion | D: `Step` fusion | E: frame borrow | G: borrowed window | change |
|---|--:|--:|--:|--:|--:|--:|--:|--:|
| `sieve-nohdr` | 54.5 | 47.5 | 44.5 | 41.9 | 41.2 | 40.8 | 40.9 | −25.0% |
| `sort-nohdr` | 74.8 | 67.0 | 64.3 | 63.6 | 62.8 | 62.2 | 62.9 | −15.9% |
| `table-nohdr` | 55.0 | 47.0 | 44.3 | 43.1 | 42.3 | 41.9 | 42.2 | −23.3% |
| `virtual-dispatch` | 69.4 | 61.5 | 58.9 | 57.8 | 56.9 | 56.5 | 57.1 | −17.7% |
| `churn-vector` | 66.9 | 58.1 | 55.2 | 54.3 | 53.9 | 53.4 | 53.8 | −19.6% |
| S7 | 66.8 | 59.6 | 57.5 | 57.0 | 55.9 | 55.7 | 56.5 | −15.4% |

Step F (the padding reclaim) does not touch `run_loop` and has no column.
Program Ir, main → G: `sieve-nohdr` 171.2 M → 142.6 M (−16.7%),
`sort-nohdr` 460.0 M → 412.1 M (−10.4%), `table-nohdr` 907.9 M →
746.7 M (−17.8%), `virtual-dispatch` 279.2 M → 245.3 M (−12.1%),
`churn-vector` 14.85 G → 13.10 G (−11.8%), S7 6.254 G → 6.118 G (−2.2%).

- **A, the fetch window** (−7 to −9 Ir). One checked read of
  `[u32; 8]` per instruction (`kbc::window`); every compiled unit is
  padded with 7 `Nop` words so the read always fits.
- **B, cold arms out of line** (−2 to −3 Ir). 48 opcodes moved into
  `#[inline(never)] Vm::run_cold`. With a wildcard arm the gain was under
  0.5%: the `match` then needed a second range check. Naming the cold
  opcodes in one arm keeps a single jump table. `run_loop` shrank from
  51.7 KB to 13.1 KB of machine code (13.6 KB after E; `run_cold` is
  22.0 KB).
- **C, in-place fusion** (−0.7 to −2.6 Ir). `Mov+Mov`,
  `JmpIfNot+Step` and `ICmp+JmpIf`/`ICmp+JmpIfNot`. The encoding does
  not change (spec/oven.md "Dispatch").
- **D, `Step` fusion** (−0.4 to −1.1 Ir). `Step+Mov` and `Step+Const`,
  the commonest pairs on S7 after C's.
- **E, frame borrow** (−0.4 to −0.6 Ir). The pure register arms index
  a local `&mut self.regs[fb..]`, so the `Vec`'s base and length are not
  reloaded after each store. The design note rejected this as worth
  only a few Ir on `Mov`; measured, it is half an Ir, kept because it
  costs nothing.
- **F, padding reclaim** (no Ir change). A put 7 `Nop` words after every
  unit: on S7, 19,867 units × 28 B = 556 KB of stream, +1.1% peak RSS in
  the first A/B. The next unit now truncates the previous unit's padding
  before its first word (`kbc::unpad`), so only the end of the stream is
  padded: 28 bytes in all, against main's stream.
- **G, borrowed window** (+0.1 to +0.8 Ir; −12% wall on
  `churn-vector`). The first A/B found `churn-vector` 2.3% *slower* than
  main at E despite 20% fewer dispatch Ir. Timing each step's binary
  placed the regression at A (main 680 ms, A 715 ms). The copied
  `[u32; 8]` was spilled to the stack (`ldp`/`stp`) and every operand
  read loaded it back, so each operand went through a store-to-load
  forward on the critical path of the next dispatch. `run_loop` now
  borrows the window in place (`kbc::window_ref`), and `run_cold` fetches
  its own instead of taking `run_loop`'s, so no window has its address
  taken; the dispatch head is one `ldr` of the op word from the stream.
  Four arms that call `&mut self` methods hoist their destination index
  first. G costs a few tenths of an Ir (some operand addresses are
  recomputed from the stream pointer) and is kept for the wall time.

On S7, 3.5 of the 55.7 Ir are `run_cold`: `VoidCast`, `Placement`,
`SetActive` and `PushCleanup` (3.4% of S7's instructions). Each of those
arms calls a memory or union walk that costs far more than the call, so
moving them back would gain under 1 Ir and grow `run_loop` again.

### A/B

Main (8b6f177ea) against the branch head, alternating: 3 pairs, each the
median of 5 runs, `/usr/bin/time -f '%e %M'`. The binaries are release
`kiln++` builds from the same toolchain. `s7`, `s8` and
`templates_wide` are `-fsyntax-only`; `accessor-wide-emit` is `-c -O2`.

| shape | main wall (s) | branch wall (s) | wall | main RSS (KB) | branch RSS (KB) | RSS | pair walls main / branch |
|---|--:|--:|--:|--:|--:|--:|---|
| `sieve-nohdr` | 0.01 | 0.01 | +0.0% | 11,980 | 12,144 | +1.37% | 0.01 0.01 0.01 / 0.01 0.01 0.01 |
| `sort-nohdr` | 0.02 | 0.02 | +0.0% | 12,216 | 12,176 | −0.33% | 0.03 0.02 0.02 / 0.02 0.03 0.02 |
| `table-nohdr` | 0.05 | 0.04 | −20.0% | 12,616 | 12,680 | +0.51% | 0.05 0.05 0.05 / 0.04 0.04 0.04 |
| `virtual-dispatch` | 0.02 | 0.02 | +0.0% | 12,344 | 12,416 | +0.58% | 0.02 0.02 0.02 / 0.02 0.02 0.02 |
| `churn-vector` | 0.64 | 0.58 | −9.4% | 12,472 | 12,352 | −0.96% | 0.65 0.64 0.64 / 0.58 0.57 0.58 |
| `fib` | 0.01 | 0.00 | — | 12,052 | 11,888 | −1.36% | 0.01 0.01 0.00 / 0.00 0.00 0.00 |
| `s7` | 0.50 | 0.50 | +0.0% | 46,712 | 46,680 | −0.07% | 0.52 0.50 0.50 / 0.50 0.50 0.50 |
| `s8` | 0.61 | 0.61 | +0.0% | 66,472 | 66,156 | −0.48% | 0.64 0.61 0.61 / 0.61 0.62 0.61 |
| `templates_wide` | 0.25 | 0.25 | +0.0% | 63,788 | 63,676 | −0.18% | 0.26 0.25 0.25 / 0.25 0.25 0.25 |
| `accessor-wide-emit` | 118.68 | 118.63 | −0.0% | 652,332 | 652,472 | +0.02% | 118.68 118.40 118.74 / 118.57 118.73 118.63 |

`/usr/bin/time` resolves 10 ms, too coarse for the small Oven shapes, so
those were also timed with `perf_counter` (3 pairs, each the median of 11
runs; ms):

| shape | main | branch | change | pair medians main / branch |
|---|--:|--:|--:|---|
| `sieve-nohdr` | 20.3 | 20.6 | +1.4% | 20.3 20.1 20.4 / 20.6 20.3 20.7 |
| `sort-nohdr` | 38.2 | 37.9 | −1.0% | 38.7 38.2 38.2 / 39.0 37.7 37.9 |
| `table-nohdr` | 74.2 | 72.9 | −1.7% | 71.8 74.2 74.4 / 72.9 73.3 72.5 |
| `virtual-dispatch` | 38.8 | 38.6 | −0.7% | 38.8 39.3 38.7 / 37.9 39.4 38.6 |
| `churn-vector` | 694.5 | 608.1 | −12.4% | 718.2 688.8 694.5 / 584.1 608.1 616.2 |
| `fib` | 11.1 | 11.1 | +0.3% | 11.1 10.9 19.3 / 11.1 11.3 11.1 |

- **Wall.** `churn-vector`, the one Oven shape whose time is mostly
  evaluation, is 9–12% faster. The other Oven shapes spend most of their
  20–75 ms starting up and parsing: `-fconstexpr-report` puts
  `sieve-nohdr`'s main evaluation at 9.9 ms on main and 7.0 ms on the
  branch, under the run-to-run spread of the whole process. S7, S8,
  `templates_wide` and `accessor-wide-emit` are unchanged.
- **RSS.** Within ±0.5% from S7 up. The 12 MB shapes move ±164 KB in
  both directions (`sieve-nohdr` +1.37%, `fib` −1.36%), reproducibly. The
  Oven's counters (`-fconstexpr-report`: bytes, peak_bytes, peak_arena,
  frozen_bytes, steps) are identical, and so is the minor-fault count
  (747 against 743 on `sieve-nohdr`); the difference is how many pages of
  the binary's text each fault maps, which moves with code layout.
- **Bytecode.** The encoding is unchanged, and after step F only the end
  of the stream is padded: 28 bytes more than main, on every program.
- **Baselines.** `oven/churn-vector` is re-recorded (671.6 ms → 580.8 ms
  against its 2026-11-03 baseline). The bench flags its RSS, 9.1 MB →
  12.1 MB (MiB), against that baseline; main is at 12.2 in the A/B above, so
  the growth is from main since that recording, not from this change. No
  other entry moved by 5%.

### Targets

| target | result |
|---|---|
| `run_loop` self Ir −25% on the Oven shapes | Met on `sieve-nohdr` (−25.0%); `table-nohdr` −23.3%, `churn-vector` −19.6%, `virtual-dispatch` −17.7%, `sort-nohdr` −15.9% |
| S7 wall −5% | Not met: unchanged. Dispatch is 14.1% of S7's Ir on main (13.2 M instructions × 66.8 Ir), so its −15.4% is −2.2% of S7's Ir |
| No shape slower beyond noise | Met |
| RSS up ≤ 1% | Met from S7 up; the 12 MB shapes move ±1.4% in both directions with identical Oven counters (above) |
| Bytecode growth < +2% of peak on S7/S8 | Met: +28 bytes |

### What remains

- **S7 is not dispatch-bound.** After this change, `run_loop` and
  `run_cold` self are 12.2% of S7's Ir. The rest of its evaluation time is
  in the memory model (`Memory::copy`'s init bits, F10; loads and stores
  through `mem.rs`) and in the cold arms' helpers (`VoidCast`,
  `Placement`, `SetActive`, `PushCleanup`, 3.8 Ir of the 56.5).
- **Per-instruction overhead left in `run_loop`.** About 40 Ir per
  instruction on the leanest shape. What is left is the jump table's
  range check (`Op` has 90 variants and the `match` cannot prove the byte
  is below 90 without `unsafe` or a 256-entry enum), a store of the op
  byte to the stack at every dispatch, and `self.regs` reloads in the arms that call
  `&mut self` helpers. Removing the range check alone is about 1 Ir.
- **Wall versus Ir.** On this host, step A lowered dispatch Ir by 13% and
  made `churn-vector` 5% slower; the cause was a stack round trip, not
  the instruction count. Later dispatch work should be judged by wall
  time on an evaluation-bound shape (`churn-vector`), not by cachegrind
  alone.
- **Not tried.** Superinstructions with a new encoding (more fused pairs
  need words the window cannot see), register allocation across
  instructions, and threaded code (excluded by the brief).

## WP-649: init-bit copy

F10 above (spec/landed/oven/WP-649.md). Main is 07d490895. Every shape
was run under `valgrind --tool=cachegrind --cache-sim=no` (with `ulimit
-v 3000000`, `timeout 1200`, `-fkiln-stack-size=256m`) with the bench
commands of spec/bench.md: the four Oven shapes `churn-vector`,
`wide-struct-read`, `wide-struct-store`, `sort-nohdr`
(`bench/oven/`), S7 and S8 (`bench/reflect/`, `-fsyntax-only
-freflection`). The release build inlines the shadow helpers into their
callers, so cost is attributed by source line, each `mem.rs` line to the
function that contains it ("self Ir" below is that sum, inlined copies
included).

### Measurement on main

Self Ir of the three functions F10 names:

| shape | program Ir | `Memory::copy` | `set_bits` | `all_bits` |
|---|--:|--:|--:|--:|
| `churn-vector` | 14,851,165,816 | 0 | 201,356,800 (1.36%) | 176,159,424 (1.19%) |
| `wide-struct-read` | 7,086,887,362 | 0 | 295,012 | 176,160,810 (2.49%) |
| `wide-struct-store` | 1,941,440,995 | 0 | 3,178,596 (0.16%) | 86,100 |
| `sort-nohdr` | 459,995,826 | 0 | 4,314,763 (0.94%) | 9,314,844 (2.02%) |
| S7 | 6,256,339,260 | 189,750 (0.003%) | 26,861,275 (0.43%) | 19,644,542 (0.31%) |

`Memory::copy` calls by length, counted with a temporary counter
(not committed). "Same phase" means source and destination have the
same offset in their 8-byte granule:

| shape | ≤ 8 B | 9–64 B | 65–512 B | > 512 B | bytes | same phase | granule-aligned | same space |
|---|--:|--:|--:|--:|--:|--:|--:|--:|
| the four Oven shapes, `templates_wide` | 0 | 0 | 0 | 0 | 0 | – | – | – |
| S7 | 28 | 1,887 | 5 | 0 | 31,443 | 1,920 | 1,920 | 1,735 |
| S8 | 1,026 | 1,126 | 5 | 0 | 22,406 | 2,157 | 2,157 | 1,528 |

So `copy` is not where the cost is. The Oven shapes make no
`Memory::copy` call: class copies of their element types are
member-wise `Load`/`Store`. S7's 1,920 copies cost about 1.2 M Ir with
the per-byte `set_bits` they made (0.02% of S7). The shadow cost is the
per-access `all_bits` (through `Space::is_init`/`is_live`) and
`set_bits` (through `set_init`), and almost every call covers one
granule. The whole of `mem.rs` is 4.3% of S7 (274.7 M Ir), whose top
sites are `check_storage` 44.6 M, `set_bits` 26.9 M, `release` 23.2 M,
`Memory::space` 22.3 M, `Ptr::decode` 21.2 M and `all_bits` 19.6 M. A
2% S7 gain from the memory model alone would need half of `mem.rs`.

### What changed

- `Memory::copy`: bit-range copy of the init map (`copy_bits`,
  `copy_bits_within`, `mem.rs:444`, `:464`). Same-phase ranges move
  whole map bytes between masked head and tail bits; others move 56-bit
  little-endian words. Cross-space pointer marks move with one
  `copy_from_slice`.
- `all_bits` / `set_bits` (`mem.rs:398`, `:416`): a range within one
  granule tests or sets one map byte under a mask; a longer one splits
  into head, whole bytes and tail (`set_bits_slow`).
- `Space::read` / `write` (`mem.rs:559`, `:579`): up to 8 bytes are one
  8-byte load or read-merge-store when 8 bytes remain in the space.

A first version (B, not kept) read and wrote every range as a `u128`
word view. Its variable `u128` shifts cost more on aarch64 than the
byte loops they replaced, and `set_bits` stopped being inlined.

### Per-step Ir

"Bitmask" is `all_bits` + `set_bits` + their helpers; "read/write" is
`Space::read` + `Space::write`:

| shape | main | B | C (kept) | main → C | bitmask main → C | read/write main → C |
|---|--:|--:|--:|--:|--:|--:|
| `churn-vector` | 14,851,165,816 | 14,721,220,413 | 14,318,446,833 | −3.6% | 419.5 M → 377.5 M (−10.0%) | 201.3 M → 167.8 M (−16.7%) |
| `wide-struct-read` | 7,086,887,362 | 7,050,066,323 | 6,914,357,087 | −2.4% | 176.5 M → 159.7 M (−9.5%) | 75.5 M → 92.3 M (+22.2%) |
| `wide-struct-store` | 1,941,440,995 | 1,941,841,470 | 1,947,725,096 | +0.3% | 3.87 M → 3.46 M (−10.7%) | 0.84 M → 0.64 M (−24.0%) |
| `sort-nohdr` | 459,995,826 | 455,547,469 | 442,689,968 | −3.8% | 14.5 M → 13.1 M (−9.8%) | 7.48 M → 6.50 M (−13.1%) |
| S7 | 6,256,339,260 | 6,243,298,226 | 6,203,623,809 | −0.8% | 51.6 M → 45.8 M (−11.2%) | 16.0 M → 12.3 M (−23.4%) |

On S7 the `copy` family fell from 189,750 Ir plus about 1 M Ir of
per-byte `set_bits` to 120,011 Ir. The per-access `set_bits` fell 33%
(26.9 M → 18.1 M) and `all_bits` 42% (19.6 M → 11.4 M), not counting
the shared `byte_mask` (8.1 M). Most of `churn-vector`'s gain is outside
`mem.rs`: `run_loop`'s self Ir fell 490.8 M once `all_bits` stopped
being inlined into it (`all_bits`' own line moved +167.8 M from
`store`), likely through lower register pressure in the loop. That
effect depends on `run_loop`'s shape. `wide-struct-read`'s read/write
rise is the same attribution move: the 8-byte load is inlined into
`Space::read`, where the old byte loop was inlined into `load`.

### A/B

Main and the branch alternating, 3 pairs, median of 5 runs per pair
under `/usr/bin/time` (wall time has 10 ms resolution):

| shape | main wall (s) | branch wall (s) | wall | main RSS (KB) | branch RSS (KB) | RSS |
|---|--:|--:|--:|--:|--:|--:|
| `churn-vector` | 0.64 | 0.64 | +0.0% | 12,472 | 12,592 | +0.96% |
| `wide-struct-read` | 0.33 | 0.32 | −3.0% | 29,664 | 29,920 | +0.86% |
| `wide-struct-store` | 0.13 | 0.13 | +0.0% | 32,784 | 32,960 | +0.54% |
| `sort-nohdr` | 0.03 | 0.02 | −33.3% | 12,216 | 12,268 | +0.43% |
| S7 | 0.50 | 0.51 | +2.0% | 46,848 | 46,940 | +0.20% |
| S8 | 0.61 | 0.61 | +0.0% | 66,060 | 66,532 | +0.71% |
| `templates_wide` | 0.25 | 0.25 | +0.0% | 63,720 | 63,800 | +0.13% |

The same shapes timed in-process (`perf_counter`), 3 alternating pairs,
median of 11 runs per pair:

| shape | main (ms) | branch (ms) | change | pair medians main / branch (ms) |
|---|--:|--:|--:|---|
| `churn-vector` | 683.8 | 686.2 | +0.3% | 686.0 666.6 683.8 / 690.0 686.2 685.7 |
| `wide-struct-read` | 378.3 | 329.4 | −12.9% | 379.1 377.9 378.3 / 340.8 326.6 329.4 |
| `wide-struct-store` | 176.0 | 174.2 | −1.0% | 176.0 177.2 174.5 / 174.2 175.7 172.5 |
| `sort-nohdr` | 38.9 | 38.4 | −1.5% | 38.9 40.1 38.4 / 38.4 38.3 38.9 |
| S7 | 534.3 | 534.4 | +0.0% | 534.3 552.2 532.4 / 534.4 546.2 532.8 |

`churn-vector`'s 3.6% fewer instructions do not show in wall time.
`wide-struct-read` is the one shape that moved by more than 5%, so it
was measured again under `cargo xtask bench --filter
oven/wide-struct-read bench/oven`, twice per side, alternating: main
333.0 and 334.7 ms, the branch 320.1 and 322.2 ms (−3.8%; the
recorded baseline is 345.4 ms). RSS moved by under 1% everywhere
(binary layout). No change reaches 5% under the bench harness, so no
baseline was re-recorded.

### What remains in the memory model

Updated after WP-650 ("WP-650: memory-access check" below has the
numbers):

- `Memory::check` / `check_storage` (about 45 Ir per access): done in
  WP-650. A cheap-first plain predicate is inlined into every access,
  and the ordered checks run only when it fails, so the reported fault
  is unchanged. The check family is −36.6% on `churn-vector` and −28.8%
  on S7.
- `Ptr::decode`, `Ptr::space`, `Memory::space` repeats: done in part
  in WP-650. A plain access no longer decodes the pointer. It still
  re-matches the space once after the check (`mem.rs:1393`, 2 Ir). The
  `decode` left is the one in `PtrAdd`'s `extent_of`.
- `Ptr::with_off`: no change needed. It is already a masked add on the
  raw word (6 Ir), with no decode or encode.
- `release`: no change needed for the shadow, because frame release
  clears none. Its cost is about 18 Ir per slot of bookkeeping (S7
  0.38%).

## WP-650: memory-access check

The first item of "What remains in the memory model" above
(spec/landed/oven/WP-650.md). The base is main 8abef735f plus WP-649.
Method as in WP-649: whole-program `valgrind --tool=cachegrind
--cache-sim=no` under `ulimit -v 3000000`, `timeout 1200` and
`-fkiln-stack-size=256m`, with the bench commands of spec/bench.md. Self
Ir is summed per `mem.rs` source line into the function that contains
it, so inlined copies count. The brief's callgrind
`--toggle-collect='*run_loop*'` was not usable. `run_loop` recurses
(a constexpr call re-enters it), so the toggle switches collection off
at the first inner return. Whole-program callgrind ran out of the 3 GB
limit on `churn-vector` and `wide-struct-read`. Call counts below come
from the code paths and the Ir of lines that run once per access, not
from call edges, because the access path is inlined.

### Measurement on main

Self Ir (M = 10^6). The first `space` is `Ptr::space`, the second
`Memory::space`. `is_live` is `Space::is_live`, called only for
lifetime-tracked storage:

| shape | program Ir | `check` | `check_storage` | `alloc_of` | `decode` | `space` | `space` | `is_live` | `with_off` | `release` | sum |
|---|--:|--:|--:|--:|--:|--:|--:|--:|--:|--:|--:|
| `churn-vector` | 12,694,967,444 | 167.8 M | 302.0 M | 100.7 M | 151.0 M | 134.2 M | 33.6 M | 0 | 100.6 M | 1,235 | 989.8 M (7.80%) |
| `wide-struct-read` | 6,167,083,148 | 58.9 M | 151.4 M | 42.1 M | 25.2 M | 50.4 M | 33.6 M | 0 | 37.8 M | 28 | 399.3 M (6.48%) |
| `wide-struct-store` | 1,937,185,694 | 2.2 M | 6.4 M | 1.8 M | 1.5 M | 2.2 M | 8,208 | 0 | 0.5 M | 16,416 | 14.6 M (0.76%) |
| `sort-nohdr` | 397,431,249 | 5.4 M | 11.2 M | 3.1 M | 4.5 M | 4.6 M | 1.8 M | 0 | 2.6 M | 1.8 M | 35.0 M (8.82%) |
| `table-nohdr` | 744,109,886 | 3.2 M | 6.4 M | 1.8 M | 3.2 M | 2.9 M | 0.9 M | 0 | 2.1 M | 14,550 | 20.6 M (2.77%) |
| S7 | 6,083,688,266 | 14.9 M | 44.6 M | 15.5 M | 20.7 M | 18.5 M | 3.6 M | 2.1 M | 4.4 M | 23.2 M | 147.6 M (2.43%) |
| `vector-alloc` | 5,610,500,065 | 32.1 M | 100.6 M | 37.3 M | 36.8 M | 38.7 M | 11.0 M | 4.4 M | 11.1 M | 99.8 M | 371.8 M (6.63%) |

`churn-vector` makes 16.78 M checked accesses (8.39 M `Load`, 8.39 M
`Store`; `Memory::space` and `space_mut` are 2 Ir each, once per access)
and 8.39 M `PtrAdd`. On main that is about 53 Ir of `check` +
`check_storage` + `alloc_of` + `decode` + `Ptr::space` per access. About
15 Ir of it is the two outlined calls returning a `Result`
through memory, and `rmw_check` (outlined from `Load`) returns it
through memory once more.

Decodes and space matches per access, from the code:

| op | main | branch, plain access |
|---|---|---|
| `Load` | 1 `decode` (`check_storage`), 2 `Ptr::space` (`alloc_of`, the result), 1 `Memory::space` (the read), +1 `Memory::space` and `is_live` if tracked | 0 full decodes: the slot's class bits pick the space and its allocation vector once; 1 `Memory::space` (the read) |
| `Store` | as `Load`, + 1 `decode` in `trial_log` (inside a trial only), `space_mut` for the write | as `Load`, `space_mut` for the write |
| `PtrAdd` | 1 `decode` + `alloc_any` in the outlined `extent_of` | the same `decode`, inlined into `ptr_add` |

### Design note

- **(a) Cheap-first check: taken.** `check_plain` is one predicate:
  `gen == raw >> GEN_SHIFT && live && flags & refuse == 0 && base !=
  UNBACKED && (raw & (OFF_MASK | PAST)) + len <= size`. Keeping the
  past-the-end bit in the offset makes a past-the-end pointer fail the
  bounds compare (bit 32 exceeds every size), so it needs no test of
  its own. Null is frame slot 0, which is never live. The
  function-pointer slot is refused by name. `refuse` always holds
  `EXTERNAL`, and every allocation whose access depends on its origin
  carries that flag, so the origin test is not needed. A false result
  runs the pre-WP-650 body unchanged (`check_ordered`), so the reported
  fault is still the one the order picks.
  
  No "plain" flag bit was added: all eight `Alloc` flag bits are in
  use, and `flags & refuse` over the existing bits does the same job.
  `Alloc` does not grow. Alone, step 1 was slower (`churn-vector`
  +1.7%): the new branch sat inside two outlined calls. Inlining the
  plain path into every caller (step 2) is what pays.
- **(b) Decode once per access: taken in part.** The plain path no
  longer builds a `Ptr`. It splits the slot bits once to pick the space
  and the allocation vector, and returns `Sp` and the absolute offset to
  the caller. The `Load`/`Store` that follows still calls
  `Memory::space(sp)`, a 2 Ir re-match. Passing `&Space` instead of `Sp`
  would mean changing the signature of every `check` caller in `vm.rs` and `vm/*.rs`, and
  `store` needs the space mutably after `check`'s shared borrow ends.
  Not worth 4 Ir per access.
- **(c) `with_off` in place: not needed.** `Ptr::with_off` already does
  a masked add and sets the past-the-end bit on the raw word, with no
  decode or encode: 6 Ir. `churn-vector`'s 100.6 M is 12 Ir per
  `PtrAdd` over its two calls. The decode and re-encode the brief
  describes are not in it.
- **(d) Bulk `release`: not taken.** `release` clears no shadow
  bitmap. Per released slot it sets `live = false`, tests `HAS_UNION`
  and pushes the slot onto the free list, about 18 Ir. That is 0.38% of
  S7 and 1.8% of `vector-alloc`. No range primitive applies.

### Per-step Ir

Program Ir per step. Each step is one commit:

1. `check_plain` + `check_ordered` (cheap-first `check_storage`).
2. `check` always inlined, with a cold `check_slow`, and `rmw_check`
   always inlined.
3. `extent_of` always inlined into `ptr_add` and `ptr_sub`.
4. The plain path also covers tracked storage whose lifetime test
   passes.

| shape | main | step 1 | step 2 | step 3 | step 4 (kept) | main → 4 |
|---|--:|--:|--:|--:|--:|--:|
| `churn-vector` | 12,694,967,444 | 12,913,067,913 | 12,246,171,663 | 11,793,267,650 | 11,793,262,099 | −7.1% |
| `wide-struct-read` | 6,167,083,148 | 6,298,060,692 | 5,953,102,850 | 5,958,273,504 | 5,961,396,076 | −3.3% |
| `sort-nohdr` | 397,431,249 | 406,323,132 | 381,664,920 | 369,975,865 | 369,968,457 | −6.9% |
| `table-nohdr` | 744,109,886 | 749,118,343 | 733,061,832 | 723,567,143 | 723,564,971 | −2.8% |
| `wide-struct-store` | 1,937,185,694 | 1,934,220,351 | 1,928,957,135 | 1,926,811,464 | 1,927,231,167 | −0.5% |
| S7 | 6,083,688,266 | 6,077,337,618 | 6,044,444,445 | 6,032,257,235 | 6,024,869,483 | −1.0% |
| `vector-alloc` | 5,610,500,065 | – | – | – | 5,492,757,245 | −2.1% |

The check family, main → step 4. It counts `check*`, `alloc_of`,
`decode`, `Ptr::space` and `Memory::space`, plus the `mem.rs` lines
cachegrind attributes to line 0:

| shape | main | step 4 | change |
|---|--:|--:|--:|
| `churn-vector` | 1,031.8 M (8.13%) | 654.3 M (5.55%) | −36.6% |
| `wide-struct-read` | 437.1 M (7.09%) | 286.1 M (4.80%) | −34.5% |
| `sort-nohdr` | 37.2 M (9.35%) | 24.3 M (6.56%) | −34.7% |
| `table-nohdr` | 22.6 M (3.04%) | 15.0 M (2.07%) | −33.7% |
| `wide-struct-store` | 15.3 M (0.79%) | 8.9 M (0.46%) | −41.5% |
| S7 | 138.3 M (2.27%) | 98.5 M (1.63%) | −28.8% |
| `vector-alloc` | 292.4 M (5.21%) | 207.0 M (3.77%) | −29.2% |

On `churn-vector`, the brief's target sum (`check` + `check_storage` +
`alloc_of` + `decode` + `Ptr::space` + `Memory::space`, without line 0)
fell from 889.2 M to 520.1 M, −41.5%. The target was −40%. The plain
check now costs about 22 Ir per access. All of the remaining `decode`
(83.9 M) and `Ptr::space` (33.6 M) is the `PtrAdd` path.

Two variants were measured and dropped:
- Testing the lifetime of tracked storage inline in `check`: +0.3% to
  +0.6% on the shapes without tracked storage.
- A `check_slow` that is not `#[cold]`: `churn-vector` +0.3%, because
  the `Load` arm grows.

S7's −3% target is out of reach on this path. The whole check family
was 2.27% of S7 on main, and S7 is mostly frontend work
(`TypeContext::view` about 5%, `malloc`/`free` about 10%).

### A/B

Main (b334807e3, which is main 8abef735f plus WP-649) was run against
the branch (step 4), alternating. Each side ran 3 pairs. Each cell is

## WP-651: pointer arithmetic

WP-650 left `ptr_add` as the largest single cost on `churn-vector`.
This section measures it, records the design picked, and checks the
result. It also bisects the peak-RSS drift of the `vector-alloc` and
`table-nohdr` baselines.

### Method

Cachegrind, as in WP-650: `--cache-sim=no`, `ulimit -v 3000000`,
`timeout 1200`, `-fkiln-stack-size=256m`, at the report's N. Self Ir is
summed per source line into the containing function, so inlined copies
count. Main is 097713e15.

### Measurement on main

The `ptr_add` columns split its self Ir by line group. "PtrAdd arm" is
the run loop's own decode for the op. "Ir/PtrAdd" is (arm + `ptr_add`)
per executed `PtrAdd`, with the count in brackets. WP-650's "about 198
Ir per `PtrAdd`" counted half the ops: `churn-vector` runs 16.78 M
`PtrAdd`, not 8.39 M.

| shape | program Ir | PtrAdd arm | type lookups | alloc lookup | index checks | division | ptr update | call/frame | `ptr_add` | Ir/PtrAdd | `ptr_sub` | `store` pro/epilogue | `space`+`space_mut` | `release` |
|---|--:|--:|--:|--:|--:|--:|--:|--:|--:|--:|--:|--:|--:|--:|
| `churn-vector` | 11,793,260,252 | 369.1 M | 553.6 M | 285.2 M | 117.4 M | 0 | 151.0 M | 553.6 M | 1660.8 M (14.08%) | 121 (16.78 M) | 0 | 142.6 M | 67.1 M | 1,235 |
| `sort-nohdr` | 369,964,647 | 9.9 M | 14.4 M | 6.5 M | 3.1 M | 0 | 3.9 M | 15.5 M | 43.5 M (11.74%) | 119 (0.45 M) | 0 | 3.1 M | 2.1 M | 1.8 M |
| `table-nohdr` | 723,567,795 | 8.1 M | 12.2 M | 5.4 M | 2.5 M | 35,916 | 3.2 M | 12.7 M | 36.0 M (4.97%) | 120 (0.37 M) | 0 | 2.0 M | 1.2 M | 23,474 |
| `vector-alloc` | 5,493,145,898 | 19.7 M | 29.5 M | 15.2 M | 6.3 M | 0 | 8.0 M | 29.5 M | 88.4 M (1.61%) | 121 (0.89 M) | 13,420 | 19.8 M | 14.6 M | 102.1 M |
| `wide-struct-read` | 5,953,646,064 | 0 | 0 | 0 | 0 | 0 | 0 | 0 | 0 (0.00%) | – (0.00 M) | 0 | 69,632 | 33.6 M | 51 |
| `s7` | 6,024,563,679 | 11.8 M | 17.8 M | 8.3 M | 3.7 M | 0.6 M | 4.6 M | 17.6 M | 52.6 M (0.87%) | 117 (0.55 M) | 0.5 M | 9.6 M | 5.5 M | 27.5 M |

`ptr_add` also calls `same_class` (67.1 M on `churn-vector`), which puts
a `PtrAdd` at about 125 Ir. The division is nearly free: almost every
`PtrAdd` steps within an array of the element type whose size is a power
of two, and the compiler divides by a shift. A third of the cost is the
call and frame (`ptr_add` returns a `Result` through memory), and a third
is the element-type lookups (`kind`, `size`, `same_unqualified`).

### Design note

- **(a) Element size at compile time: taken.** The compiler knows each
  `PtrAdd`'s element `OTy`. When the element's size is a power of two
  and can no longer change (the type is not an incomplete class's
  `Opaque` slot), and the index is not unsigned 128-bit, it writes
  log2(size) + 1 into bits 10..=15 of the op's aux (`kbc::ptr_add_aux`).
  The PtrAdd encoding keeps its four operands, so the bytecode does not
  grow. The run loop then does an inline step:
  - one slot lookup (`Memory::alloc_raw`: the slot's class bits pick the
    space, plus a generation compare);
  - one type-table read to see whether the allocation is an array of
    exactly the element type (`OTy`s are cv-unqualified, so identity is
    `same_unqualified`);
  - a shift for the current index;
  - one i128 compare of the new index against `0..=n`.

  The allocation's type is a run-time property, so that one type read
  stays. Everything else goes to a cold `ptr_add_op` → `ptr_add`.
  `ptr_add` tries the same step with the size read from the type table,
  so arrays whose element size is not a power of two skip the old body
  too. Otherwise it runs the pre-WP-651 body (`ptr_add_slow`), which
  steps within the extent of the subobject. Results and faults are
  therefore the old body's. A unit test
  (`ptr_add_agrees_with_the_reference`) checks every path against the
  old body, kept as a test-only reference, over (pointer, index, element
  size) triples. The triples cover:
  - in bounds, one past the end, and beyond;
  - negative indices into bounds and beyond;
  - i128 overflow;
  - zero, odd and power-of-two element sizes;
  - every index width, signedness and negate bit.

  Disabling the element-type test makes it fail.
- **(b) Division-free check: taken, as a shift.** The current index is
  `off >> k` and the new offset is `new << k`, which is cheaper than the
  brief's multiply-and-compare and faults on the same inputs. The sum is
  formed in i128 with wrapping arithmetic. Only an index near the i128
  limits can wrap, and it then fails the range test and takes the
  general path. The division remains only on the cold path, for element
  sizes that are not a power of two.
- **(c) Inline `store`: not taken.** The prologue and epilogue are 1.2%
  of `churn-vector`, 0.84% of `sort-nohdr` and at most 0.36% of the other
  shapes. `space`/`space_mut` add 0.57% of `churn-vector`. The run loop
  is register-bound: adding the inline step alone cost
  `wide-struct-read`, which runs no `PtrAdd`, +0.56% Ir in unrelated
  arms. Left as a follow-up.
- **(d) Bulk `release`: not taken.** It is 1.86% of `vector-alloc` and
  0.46% of S7, and the work is per slot (WP-650 (d)).
- **Considered and dropped:**
  - outlining the inline step into an `#[inline(never)]` function: about
    55 Ir per `PtrAdd`, and worse on every shape (s4 below);
  - 64-bit index arithmetic: it saves about 2 Ir per op and needs a
    second overflow argument.

### Per-step Ir

One commit lands the design. The columns are the variants measured on
the way to it:
- s1: an aux field (0, or shift + 1), with the index-width test at run
  time and `wrapping_neg` for negate.
- s2: eligibility folded into the field (a reserved value means "read
  the size inline"), with negate as `wrapping_sub`.
- s3 (kept): only power-of-two shifts inline, with the size read on the
  cold path. The shift serves both the index and the new offset, and the
  two paths merge as a `u64` in a register rather than a `Result` through
  memory.
- s4: s3 with the step outlined.

| shape | main | s1 | s2 | s3 (kept) | s4 (dropped) |
|---|--:|--:|--:|--:|--:|
| churn-vector | 11,793,260,252 | 10,736,397,505 | 10,719,603,032 | 10,602,166,026 (−10.10%) | 10,920,922,365 |
| sort-nohdr | 369,964,647 | 341,896,769 | 341,380,136 | 338,274,076 (−8.57%) | 347,374,072 |
| table-nohdr | 723,567,795 | 708,908,649 | 708,412,947 | 705,931,336 (−2.44%) | 709,827,086 |
| vector-alloc | 5,493,145,898 | 5,443,489,316 | 5,441,311,195 | 5,435,246,198 (−1.05%) | – |
| wide-struct-read | 5,953,646,064 | 5,990,845,517 | 5,990,785,283 | 5,987,294,252 (+0.56%) | 5,995,409,142 |
| s7 | 6,024,563,679 | 6,019,684,108 | 6,018,449,383 | 6,015,322,370 (−0.15%) | – |

The `PtrAdd` cost in each shape, main → s3. Main is the arm plus
`ptr_add` plus `same_class`. s3 is the arm plus the run loop's growth
plus whatever still reaches `ptr_add`/`ptr_add_op`/`same_class`:

| shape | main | s3 | change | Ir/PtrAdd main → s3 |
|---|--:|--:|--:|--:|
| `churn-vector` | 2,097.0 M | 905.8 M | −56.8% | 125 → 54 |
| `sort-nohdr` | 55.1 M | 23.4 M | −57.5% | 122 → 52 |
| `table-nohdr` | 45.6 M | 27.9 M | −38.8% | 123 → 75 |
| `vector-alloc` | 112.2 M | 53.8 M | −52.0% | 126 → 60 |
| S7 | 67.6 M | 57.3 M | −15.2% | 123 → 104 |

On `churn-vector`, everything `ptr_add` did (`ptr_add` + `same_class`,
1,727.9 M) is replaced by 536.8 M of run-loop growth, −68.9%. The brief
targeted −60%, or at most 80 Ir per `PtrAdd`, and the result is 54. No
`PtrAdd` on `churn-vector` or `sort-nohdr` leaves the inline step. In S7
and `table-nohdr`, the calls that remain are the ones the inline step
does not cover: steps within a subobject, and elements whose size is not
a power of two. They take the general path as before.

`wide-struct-read` (+0.56%, 33.6 M) runs no `PtrAdd`. The growth is
register allocation in the run loop: the `IntArith` result write
(`vm.rs:857`) gained 3 Ir per op. Outlining the step (s4) did not
remove it. The A/B below shows it within noise.

Program output (diagnostics and stderr) is byte-identical to main on all
six shapes.

### Baseline drift

The `vector-alloc` and `table-nohdr` baselines were recorded on 11-03 by
e46fe36ee, merged as 2db5dfc55: 36,336 KB and 9,316 KB. Since then both
report `REGRESSED (mem)` under every binary (WP-650 above). Release
builds of main commits were measured by peak RSS, median of 3 under the
harness's command line, with an empty TU added as a control:

| binary | empty TU | table-nohdr | vector-alloc |
|---|--:|--:|--:|
| 2db5dfc55 | 6504 | 9564 | 36104 |
| 1fe2ec39d | 7732 | 10664 | 37628 |
| 7b9507098 | 7236 | 10732 | 37560 |
| d5c242a42 (c7269ab8d~1) | 7560 | 11084 | 37588 |
| c7269ab8d | 9568 | 13188 | 39924 |
| main | 9216 | 12764 | 38556 |

The empty TU moves with the two shapes, so the drift is a fixed cost of
starting the binary, not Oven memory. Massif agrees: the heap peak on
the empty TU is 364,666 bytes on 2db5dfc55 and 368,979 on main.

Most of the drift is one commit. c7269ab8d ("sema, codegen, driver: x86
target builtins and -march=<cpu> features") adds about 2.0 MB to every
compile. The cause is that kiln++ is a PIE:
- its `R_AARCH64_RELATIVE` relocations (`.rela.dyn`) are read at
  startup;
- every page of `.data.rel.ro` they patch is dirtied.

The commit grows `.rela.dyn` from 717,888 to 1,753,608 bytes,
`.data.rel.ro` from 598,496 to 1,566,992, and `.rodata` from 1,287,420
to 1,639,596. The x86 builtin rows are a `const` slice
(`crates/kiln_ast/src/x86_builtins.rs:11`) reached through `all()`
(`crates/kiln_ast/src/decl.rs:5666`). Each crate that inlines `all()`
gets its own copy, so the binary holds the 155,760-byte table twice,
6,384 relocations each, with a `&str` pointer per name and signature.

The rest of the drift, about 1.1 to 1.2 MB, is spread across the 768
commits from 2db5dfc55 to 1fe2ec39d. That range also spans the switch of
toolchain (1.99.1 → 1.95, WP-614). It is broad binary growth with no
single step, and it was not bisected further within the time box.

This is not an Oven regression, and the fix lies outside `kiln_oven`, so
it is a follow-up:
- make `X86` a `static`, which keeps one copy;
- store the strings as offsets into one `&str` rather than as pointers,
  so they need no relocation;
- or link with packed relative relocations (`-z pack-relative-relocs`).

The two baselines are re-recorded on this branch (below), with this
section as the reason.

Each cell is the median of 5 runs under `/usr/bin/time -f '%e %M'`, and
wall time has 10 ms resolution. `accessor-wide-emit` (`-c -O2`) got 1 run
per pair.

### What remains

- `crates/kiln_oven/src/vm.rs:2907` `store` and `mem.rs:1414`/`:1422`

  above. They were measured here (design note (c) and (d)) and left.
- `vm.rs:2263` `ptr_add_slow` still takes steps within a subobject
  (class members, array rows reached through a member pointer). These
  are S7's and `table-nohdr`'s remaining `PtrAdd` cost, about 104 and 75
  Ir per op.
- `vm.rs:857` the `IntArith` arm. Its result write lost a register to
  the new step: +3 Ir per op on `wide-struct-read`, +0.56% Ir and not
  visible in wall time.
- `crates/kiln_ast/src/x86_builtins.rs:11` `X86` was a `const` slice,
  and the baseline drift above follows from it. Done in WP-652 below:
  the table is plain data, and every `bench/oven` baseline is
  re-recorded.

## WP-652: start-up RSS

The WP-651 "Baseline drift" follow-up. Every kiln++ compile, the empty
TU included, started about 2.5 MB bigger in peak RSS than on 11-03
(2db5dfc55), and about 2 MB of that was the x86 builtin table WP-630
added. The spec is spec/landed/sema/WP-652.md.

### Measurement

Release build (the profile `cargo xtask bench` builds), the harness's
command line (`-std=c++26 -fsyntax-only <//@ args>
-fconstexpr-report=<file>`). RSS is the median of 5 runs under
`/usr/bin/time -f '%e %M'`, in KB. Section sizes are in bytes, from
`readelf -S`. Relocations are the `R_AARCH64_RELATIVE` count from
`readelf -r`. Copies counts the runs of relocated data whose pointers
lead to the same bytes as the table's: identical blocks of rows in
`.data.rel.ro`.

| binary | empty TU | table-nohdr | vector-alloc | .rela.dyn | .data.rel.ro | .rodata | RELATIVE relocs | table copies |
|---|--:|--:|--:|--:|--:|--:|--:|--:|
| 2db5dfc55 (11-03) | 6728 | 9596 | 37088 | 379,248 | 334,464 | 1,052,561 | 15,789 | 0 |
| main 3d1afcb1c | 9204 | 12728 | 39420 | 1,606,608 | 1,416,160 | 1,591,604 | 66,929 | 4 |
| `static` | 8356 | 11452 | 38344 | 1,146,936 | 948,856 | 1,440,084 | 47,776 | 1 |
| plain data (this branch) | 7860 | 11484 | 38312 | 993,816 | 793,176 | 1,479,172 | 41,396 | 0 (in `.rodata`) |

`size` (text / data): main 13,149,267 / 1,453,664; `static`
12,537,279 / 986,360; branch 12,425,007 / 830,688. Wall time does not
move (0.04 s on `table-nohdr`, 0.37 to 0.38 s on `vector-alloc` for
every binary).

On main the table was in the binary four times, not twice. Each crate
or codegen unit that inlined an accessor (`all()`, `row()`, `by_name`)
got its own copy of the `const`. Each copy was
155,760 bytes of `.data.rel.ro` plus about 6,386 relocations (153 KB of
`.rela.dyn`). The loader reads all of `.rela.dyn` and writes every
patched page at start-up, so that is about 1.2 MB of binary touched
before `main`.

### The fix

1. **`static`** (one copy): the empty TU drops by 848 KB. There are
   still about 6,400 relocations, one per `&str` or slice in a row.
2. **Plain data** (no pointers). `scripts/gen-x86-builtins.py` now
   emits three statics:
   - `STRS`, one `&str` holding the names in row order, then each
     distinct prototype and features string once (about 63 KB);
   - `IMMS`, the distinct immediate-check lists;
   - `X86`, 1,947 rows of 20 bytes each, holding `u32`/`u8`
     offset/length pairs into `STRS` and `IMMS`, the attributes and the
     header mark.

   The rows are `.rodata` with no relocation. The empty TU drops by a
   further 496 KB, 1,344 KB in all. The generator's `--reemit` rewrote
   the table from its existing rows, without probing clang.

The stop rule was "the table's cost is under 100 KB". Its start-up cost
is now one relocation, and its pages are read only when a lookup touches
them, so the rule is met. `by_name` is still a binary search, and an id
is still an index. `row` and `builtin::info` now decode the row and
return it by value. The generator and the decoder are about 270 lines.

### Bench before/after

`cargo xtask bench bench/oven` was run with main's binary and the
branch's, alternating, 2 rounds each, without recording. Load was 6.3
to 7.6. Each cell is the entry's median of 5 runs; both rounds are
shown.

The baselines were then re-recorded with the branch binary (load 6.9),
with the note "startup RSS drift (WP-652)". That covers all 23 entries:
- 18 reported `REGRESSED (mem)`, `churn-string` with `(bytes)` too;
- the other 5 reported `improved`, because their baselines were
  recorded on 11-05 and 11-06 with the drift included.

No entry's time regressed against its old baseline, so none was held
back. `vector-alloc` is +0.3%, within its 2.3% spread. A run after the
recording is clean: 0 regressed, 0 improved.

counter, 0 → 8. It was not RSS, and it comes from an earlier change
(the steps, `bytes` and `peak_bytes` are identical). The re-recording
takes it.

### What remains

The empty TU is 7,860 KB against 6,728 KB on 2db5dfc55 (+1,132 KB);
the bench entries are about 1.9 MB over their 11-03 baselines. That
matches the binary's remaining relocation growth since 2db5dfc55:
`.rela.dyn` +614,568 bytes and `.data.rel.ro` +458,712. The largest
known piece:
- `crates/kiln_ast/src/neon_builtins.rs:11` `NEON` is a `const &[Row]`
  with the same shape, also in four copies (about 7,200 duplicate
  relocations). Making it `static` was measured at −500 KB on the empty
  TU (7,360 KB), but it is not in this package. The table is
  `@generated` by `scripts/gen-neon-builtins.py:107`, which needs a
  clang build directory to run. The `--reemit` approach used here
  applies to it.

Other candidates:
- link with packed relative relocations (`-z pack-relative-relocs`),
  which shrinks `.rela.dyn` for every remaining pointer;
- the `pub const` builtin tables in `crates/kiln_ast/src/decl.rs` (the
  `ALL` and libm lists) may be duplicated the same way. They were not
  measured.

## WP-653: NEON table and start-up relocations

The WP-652 "What remains" follow-up. Every kiln++ compile still started
about 1.3 MB bigger in peak RSS than on 11-03 (2db5dfc55). This WP makes
the NEON builtin table plain data, makes the generic builtin `Info`
lists `static`, and links `kiln` with packed relative relocations. The
spec is spec/landed/sema/WP-653.md.

### Measurement

The method is WP-652's:
- release build, the harness's command line;
- RSS is the median of 5 runs under `/usr/bin/time -f '%e %M'`, in KB;
- sections are in bytes (`readelf -S`), relocations are the
  `R_AARCH64_RELATIVE` count (`readelf -r`);
- copies are found by decoding each relocated pointer to the string it
  points at, then counting the blocks of `.data.rel.ro` whose pointers
  spell a table's rows in order.

The columns are cumulative: each step's binary includes the steps above
it.

| binary | empty TU | table-nohdr | vector-alloc | .rela.dyn | .relr.dyn | .data.rel.ro | .rodata | RELATIVE relocs | NEON copies |
|---|--:|--:|--:|--:|--:|--:|--:|--:|--:|
| 2db5dfc55 (11-03) | 6728 | 9604 | 37132 | 379,248 | 0 | 334,464 | 1,052,561 | 15,789 | 0 |
| main 1a3b8deca | 8056 | 11776 | 38004 | 997,104 | 0 | 795,624 | 1,480,228 | 41,533 | 4 |
| NEON plain data | 7152 | 10760 | 37192 | 685,776 | 0 | 553,448 | 1,407,084 | 28,561 | 0 (in `.rodata`) |
| `Info` lists `static` | 7152 | 10760 | 37192 | 685,776 | 0 | 553,448 | 1,407,084 | 28,561 | 0 |
| RELR (this branch) | 6944 | 10264 | 36644 | 312 | 8,832 | 553,448 | 1,407,084 | 28,561 packed | 0 |

`size` (text / data): main 12,547,219 / 833,136; NEON plain data and
`Info` static 12,175,819 / 590,968; 11-03 10,294,808 / 372,096. Wall
time does not move: 0.00 s on the empty TU, 0.04 s on `table-nohdr`,
0.36 to 0.38 s on `vector-alloc`, for every binary.

**NEON.** On main the table was in the binary four times, like the x86
table before WP-652. Each copy held 1,081 rows of 56 bytes in
`.data.rel.ro` (60,536 bytes) and 3,243 relocations (77,832 bytes of
`.rela.dyn`), 12,972 relocations in all. The plain-data table is one
`.rodata` copy: `NEON` is 1,081 rows of 16 bytes (17,296 bytes) and
`STRS` is 30,561 bytes. The empty TU drops by 904 KB.

**`Info` lists.** Each of the fifteen `pub const` lists in
`crates/kiln_ast/src/decl.rs` was already in the binary once. A row is
40 bytes of `.data.rel.ro` and 2 relocations (48 bytes of `.rela.dyn`):

| list | rows | copies on main | .data.rel.ro | .rela.dyn |
|---|--:|--:|--:|--:|
| `ALL` | 124 | 1 | 4,960 | 5,952 |
| `CORO` | 14 | 1 | 560 | 672 |
| `LIB` | 232 | 1 | 9,280 | 11,136 |
| `INT_MEM` | 26 | 1 | 1,040 | 1,248 |
| `LIBM` | 193 | 1 | 7,720 | 9,264 |
| `ATOMIC` | 47 | 1 | 1,880 | 2,256 |
| `SYNC` | 107 | 1 | 4,280 | 5,136 |
| `CHECKED` | 36 | 1 | 1,440 | 1,728 |
| `EXTRA` | 27 | 1 | 1,080 | 1,296 |
| `F128` | 84 | 1 | 3,360 | 4,032 |
| `FLOATN` | 15 | 1 | 600 | 720 |
| `F16_INTR` | 14 | 1 | 560 | 672 |
| `F16_LIBM` | 20 | 1 | 800 | 960 |
| `C_MATH_INTR` | 12 | 1 | 480 | 576 |
| `C_MATH_LIB` | 9 | 1 | 360 | 432 |
| total | 960 | | 38,400 | 46,080 |

No list had more than one copy, so the brief's plain-data rule did not
apply to any of them. The change is `const` → `static`, the smallest one.
The binary is identical in every section. It keeps them at one copy if
another crate starts inlining an accessor (`LIB`, `INT_MEM` and `LIBM`
are already read from kiln_sema).

**RELR.** With `-z pack-relative-relocs` the 28,561 relative
relocations are a 8,832-byte `.relr.dyn` bitmap; `.rela.dyn` keeps 13
non-relative entries (312 bytes). The loader no longer reads 670 KB of
relocation records at start-up. It still writes every patched page of
`.data.rel.ro`, because the pointers are still there. The empty TU drops
by a further 208 KB, `table-nohdr` by 496 KB.

The flag needs:
- GNU ld 2.38+ (the image's is 2.44), lld 15+ (Debian's 19.1.7) or
  rust-lld (Rust 1.95's, used by default on x86_64 Linux). Each was
  checked locally on a small C program;
- glibc 2.36+ at run time. The binary gains a `GLIBC_ABI_DT_RELR`
  version need; it already needs `GLIBC_2.39`. Both CI jobs run in the
  `gcc:16.1.0-trixie` image (glibc 2.41).

Decision (updated): shipped in #302 as its own commit, then **reverted by
WP-662**: the binary failed to start on the first machine outside this
container it was run on (`version 'GLIBC_ABI_DT_RELR' not found`, a glibc
older than 2.36), and a run-time platform requirement is not worth
0.2–0.5 MB of start-up RSS. The `bench/oven` baselines were re-recorded
without it. Originally: shipped, as its own commit, so it can be dropped alone. It is
a `cargo:rustc-link-arg-bin=kiln=` line in `crates/kiln_driver/build.rs`
for Linux targets, so it changes no dependency's build and invalidates
no cache. aarch64 is checked here (`cargo xtask ci --quick` plus the compat-19 pass green). The x86_64
runner is only checked by proxy: rust-lld and lld accept the flag, and
x86_64 CI runs only on a PR. If that job fails at link time, drop that
commit.

### A/B

main 1a3b8deca against the branch, alternating, 3 pairs. Each pair is
the median of 5 runs, under `/usr/bin/time -f '%e %M'`, at load 6.1 to
7.9. The shapes and their command lines:
- `empty`: an empty TU;
- `table-nohdr`, `vector-alloc`, `churn-vector`: the bench/oven entries;
- `templates_wide`: bench/frontend/templates_wide.cpp, `-fsyntax-only`;
- `accessor-wide-emit`: bench/frontend/accessor-wide-emit.cpp,
  `-S -emit-llvm`;
- `s8n200`: bench/reflect/s8.cpp at N=200.

RSS is in KB. Wall is in seconds, the median of the three pairs.

| shape | main RSS (3 pairs) | branch RSS (3 pairs) | Δ median | main wall | branch wall |
|---|--:|--:|--:|--:|--:|
| `empty` | 8072, 8072, 8072 | 6948, 6944, 6940 | −1,128 (−14.0%) | 0.00 | 0.00 |
| `table-nohdr` | 11772, 11772, 11776 | 10264, 10264, 10264 | −1,508 (−12.8%) | 0.04 | 0.04 |
| `vector-alloc` | 38132, 38068, 38084 | 36612, 36632, 36612 | −1,472 (−3.9%) | 0.37 | 0.37 |
| `churn-vector` | 11772, 11768, 11780 | 10740, 10740, 10740 | −1,032 (−8.8%) | 0.46 | 0.46 |
| `templates_wide` | 62084, 61384, 61048 | 60308, 61152, 60396 | −988 (−1.6%) | 0.22 | 0.23 |
| `accessor-wide-emit` | 140520, 140364, 140404 | 140404, 140416, 140472 | +12 (0.0%) | 1.47 | 1.46 |
| `s8n200` | 171916, 170668, 171368 | 170720, 168104, 170064 | −1,304 (−0.8%) | 1.81 | 1.81 |

The empty TU is 1,128 KB smaller; the target was 900 KB. No shape is
slower.
- `templates_wide`'s 0.22 → 0.23 is the 10 ms resolution of `%e`.
  Timed with a finer clock (15 alternating runs), it is 231.2 ms on main
  and 227.2 ms on the branch, both with a minimum of 220.7 ms.
- `accessor-wide-emit` does not move because its peak is not kiln++'s.
  `-S -emit-llvm` pipes kiln++'s IR through `opt -passes=default<O0>`
  and `opt -S` (`crates/kiln_driver/src/codegen_cmd.rs:130`), and
  `/usr/bin/time` reports the largest RSS in the process tree. That is
  `opt`, at 140 MB. kiln++'s own peak is about 85 MB, and polling
  `/proc` shows it is 1.1 MB smaller in `RssFile` on the branch, as
  everywhere else.

### What remains

The empty TU is 6,944 KB against 6,728 KB on 2db5dfc55 (+216 KB; 7,152 KB
without RELR after WP-662's revert, +424 KB). With RELR the loader would
no longer read `.rela.dyn`; without it `.rela.dyn` (685,776 bytes) is read
at every start, and the rest of the drift is mostly the pages of `.data.rel.ro` that the relocations patch:
553,448 bytes against 334,464 (+219 KB), in 28,561 relocations against
15,789. By the strings the relocated pointers lead to, the growth since
11-03 is:
- `crates/kiln_codegen/src/x86_intrinsics.rs:10` `MAP`. It is a
  `pub(crate) const &[Map]` of 1,129 rows (72 bytes each), in one copy.
  Each row holds four `&str`: the builtin, the `llvm.x86.*` intrinsic,
  its return type and its parameter types. That is about 4,500
  relocations and 81 KB of `.data.rel.ro`, the largest piece. It is
  `@generated` by `scripts/gen-x86-builtins.py`, and the plain-data
  layout of the builtin tables applies to it.
- `crates/kiln_driver/src/x86_targets.rs:19` `CPUS` and `:97` `IMPLIES`.
  These are `const` tables of per-CPU feature and macro lists (`__AVX__`,
  `sse4.2`, the `"1"` values), about 4,000 relocations.
- Panic locations: 3,306 → 3,829 (+523). This is code growth, not a
  table.

Making `MAP`, `CPUS` and `IMPLIES` plain data would close most of the
remaining ~220 KB. They were not in this package.

## WP-654: IR printer

F4 and F18. R3 spends 11.1% of its frontend in `kiln_llvm` printing
textual IR, and SipHash in the interning tables is another 1.1%. This
WP makes the printer cheaper and swaps the tables to Fx. It also sizes
the bitcode writer, which it does not start. The spec is
spec/landed/codegen/WP-654.md.

### Measurement on main

Main is 6f8937391. The command line is the harness's
`-std=c++26 -S -emit-llvm -o /dev/null`, under cachegrind
(`--cache-sim=no`). Each cell is self Ir summed into its containing
functions, in millions.

The "fmt::Write adapter" row is `String`/`Formatter` `write_str` and
`write_char`, `core::fmt::write`, `pad_integral` and `fmt::num`. Part of
it comes from `format!` outside the printer: about 5 M on every shape,
as `simd-generate` shows. The SipHash and hashbrown callers come from
callgrind (`hash_one`, inclusive):
- on `accessor-wide-emit`, `AttrTable::intern` 106 M,
  `TypeTable::intern` 37 M and `ConstTable::intern` 37 M;
- on `virtual-lattice-emit`, `ConstTable::intern` 77 M and
  `TypeTable::intern` 33 M, with the comdat and global-name maps after
  them.

All of these are kiln_llvm. kiln_codegen already used `FxHashMap`: its
only std `HashMap` is a test helper. The hashbrown row is mostly the
rest of the compiler's Fx tables.

| main, M Ir | accessor-wide-emit | registration-wide-obj | virtual-lattice-emit | simd-generate |
|---|--:|--:|--:|--:|
| names (`write_llvm_name`, `write_md_ident`) | 64.0 | 86.4 | 12.4 | 0.0 |
| escaping (`write_escaped`) | 0.2 | 0.5 | 0.0 | 0.0 |
| constants, ints, floats | 2.4 | 10.2 | 3.3 | 0.0 |
| types (`TypeTable::write*`, `Printer::ty`) | 20.0 | 47.4 | 18.7 | 0.0 |
| attributes (`Attr::write`, `attr_set`) | 10.8 | 16.0 | 0.2 | 0.0 |
| metadata | 1.3 | 1.9 | 0.0 | 0.0 |
| instructions, operands | 91.7 | 250.7 | 20.4 | 0.0 |
| module/function/global framing, slots, `TypeFinder` | 62.5 | 174.2 | 6.3 | 0.0 |
| fmt::Write adapter | 298.6 | 640.3 | 106.2 | 5.0 |
| &nbsp;&nbsp;of which `write_char` | 101.1 | 170.6 | 12.4 | 0.0 |
| **printing total** | **551.5** (5.38%) | **1,227.7** (10.40%) | **167.5** (9.77%) | 5.1 (0.09%) |
| SipHasher13 | 104.4 (1.02%) | 222.8 (1.89%) | 62.3 (3.63%) | 2.7 (0.05%) |
| hashbrown/HashMap internals | 91.1 (0.89%) | 104.4 (0.88%) | 56.2 (3.27%) | 92.4 (1.59%) |
| shape total | 10,243.3 | 11,808.0 | 1,714.9 | 5,798.8 |
| printed size, bytes | 15,554,534 | 26,747,623 | 4,282,150 | 530 |

**`opt` on text against bitcode.** The text was printed with
`-fclang-compat=19`, so that `opt-19` and `llvm-as-19` accept it. Each
time is the median of 5 runs, in seconds; sizes are in bytes.
- `opt -O2` is `opt-19 -passes='default<O2>'`, timed on the text and on
  `llvm-as` bitcode of the same text.
- Parse is `opt-19 -passes=verify -disable-output`, which is the parse
  cost alone.
- `-c -O2` is kiln++'s whole wall at `-c -O2`.

| shape | text | bitcode | `opt -O2` text | `opt -O2` bc | parse text | parse bc | saving | `-c -O2` wall | saving / wall |
|---|--:|--:|--:|--:|--:|--:|--:|--:|--:|
| accessor-wide-emit | 16,019,866 | 3,408,392 | 45.56 | 43.48 | 0.350 | 0.198 | 0.152 | 118.85 | 0.13% |
| registration-wide-obj | 31,219,855 | 7,548,800 | 4.88 | 4.49 | 0.793 | 0.535 | 0.257 | 7.00 | 3.7% |
| virtual-lattice-emit | 4,299,658 | 532,500 | 0.13 | 0.09 | 0.066 | 0.030 | 0.036 | 0.39 | 9.2% |
| simd-generate | 524 | 1,844 | 0.00 | 0.00 | — | — | — | 0.49 | — |

Across the shapes the saving is 0.445 s of 126.7 s, 0.35%. Only
`virtual-lattice-emit` passes 5%, and there it is 36 ms. The
production `-c -O2` wall is `opt` and `llc` (on `accessor-wide-emit`, `llc` is
about 70 s and `opt` about 45 s; kiln++'s own work is 1.6 s).

### Design note

**(a) Printer.**
- **Tokens.** The printer writes whole `&str` tokens into a concrete
  `String`, not into `&mut dyn fmt::Write`, so no character costs a
  dynamic call. `Module::print` (io) and `write_to` (fmt) fill that
  `String` and flush it in chunks of at least 64 KiB, at global,
  function and metadata boundaries. The `IoAdapter` is gone.
- **Pre-sizing.** `to_text` pre-sizes the `String` from a count pass
  (`text_size_hint`: instructions, functions, globals and metadata
  times a per-item estimate). The brief's "previous module's size" does
  not apply, because the driver prints one module per process.
- **Caches.**
  - Types and inline attribute sets: their text is cached in one
    per-module arena `String` with `(offset, len)` spans indexed by id,
    filled lazily on first use.
  - Global and function names: each name is already stored once in
    the module, so a second copy would add memory for nothing. Instead
    the quoting decision is cached per global as a `Cell<Quote>`:
    `Plain`, `Quoted` (verbatim inside quotes), or `Escaped`.
  - Locals: nothing is cached. Most locals are `%N` slots, and a named
    local is one table lookup per byte.
- **Integers** go through a local two-digit itoa. **Floats** keep their
  existing path: constants are 0.02% to 0.2% of the shapes.

**(b) F18.** Every `HashMap`/`HashSet` in kiln_llvm becomes
`kiln_source::fx::FxHashMap`/`FxHashSet` (24 sites in `attr`,
`constant`, `function`, `module` and `types`). None of them is iterated:
- the printed order comes from the `Vec`s the tables index;
- name uniquing probes the map, then appends a counter.

So no order needs keeping. kiln_codegen needs no change.

**(c) Bitcode writer: NO-GO.** The saving is 0.13% (accessor), 3.7%
(registration) and 9.2% (virtual-lattice, 36 ms) of the `-c -O2` wall,
and 0.35% in aggregate, against the 5% bar. Skipping the text round
trip also saves the printing itself, but that is now 2.6% to 6.1% of the
frontend's Ir, and under 1% of `-c -O2` on any shape with real
optimization work. If it is ever revisited, a minimal LLVM 19 writer for
kiln's IR needs:
- the bitstream core (fixed and VBR fields, blocks, no abbreviations):
  about 250 lines;
- the identification block, and the module block (version, triple,
  datalayout, source filename, global variable, function, alias and
  comdat records);
- the type table, the paramattr group and paramattr blocks;
- module- and function-level constants, including constant expressions
  and GEPs;
- metadata (strings, nodes, kinds, attachments);
- function blocks (relative value ids, about 60 instruction records,
  call attributes, operand bundles, sync scopes);
- the value symbol table, strtab and symtab;
- tests that round-trip through `llvm-dis` against the text printer.

That is about 4,000 lines (L), plus a second encoding that would have to
track every printer change.

### Per-step Ir

Each step was measured with the same cachegrind command line on the
release binary, in millions of Ir:
1. **Step 1** (8cd067609): whole-token writes, the concrete `String`,
   chunked io and fmt, pre-sizing, run-based escaping, local itoa.
2. **Step 2** (a54fb4fd2): the type and attribute-set text arena, and
   per-global quoting flags.
3. **Step 3** (a7a82bc1a): Fx tables.
4. **Step 4** (41e30d2c1): verbatim quoted names, the two-digit itoa,
   and a borrow-only fast path for cached types.

The `-S -emit-llvm` text of every step is byte-identical to main on the
four shapes.

**Targets.**
- Printing Ir: **met**, −53.3% on `accessor-wide-emit`.

  1,101.

### What remains

- **Done in WP-655.** `crates/kiln_llvm/src/types.rs:502` `TypeTable::is_sized` is 156.6 M
  self Ir on `accessor-wide-emit` (1.6%, the largest kiln_llvm function
  there). It walks the whole type on every call, and the builder calls
  it on each alloca, load, store and GEP (`builder.rs:482`, `:507`,
  `:541`, `:1327`). A sized bit computed when the type is interned (or
  when a named body is set) would remove it.
- `AttrTable::intern` (40.6 M on accessor, 75.9 M on registration) and
  `IrBuilder::insert` (30.6 M, 79.2 M) are now the largest kiln_llvm
  costs outside printing.
- Printing that remains is `Printer::instruction`/`operand`/`module`
  (about 35 M each on accessor) and `write_u64` for `%N` slots. The next
  cut would be caching each function's local slot text, which is about
  10% more of what is left.
- Bitcode: NO-GO, as above. The production `-c -O2` wall is in `opt`'s
  passes and `llc`, not in parsing.

## WP-655: declaration and type queries

This WP covers F7 and F8, plus the `TypeTable::is_sized` walk that WP-654
left open. `TypeContext::view` (37 Ir) and `DeclArena::redecl` (48 Ir)
are both O(1). Their cost is how often they are called. The spec is
spec/landed/ast/WP-655.md.

### Measurement on main

Main is 090e033e4. The shapes and flags are:
- `bench/frontend/templates_wide.cpp` (tw), `templates_deep.cpp` (td),
  `nested-template-ids.cpp` (nt) and `accessor-wide-emit.cpp` (aw):
  `-std=c++26 -fsyntax-only`, except aw, which uses
  `-S -emit-llvm -o /dev/null`;
- S8 at N=100 (`cargo xtask gen-reflect --shape s8 --n 100`):
  `-freflection`, libstdc++ from GCC 16.1.

Each shape ran under callgrind with `--cache-sim=no`, `ulimit -v 3000000`
and `timeout 1200`.

Where the measurements come from:
- **Self Ir** is the function's own cost plus the cost of its copies
  inlined into callers, counted by source line.
- **Calls** are exact counts from the temporary build described
  below. Callgrind's own call counts run 1.7 to 1.9 times higher on this
  host. The exception is `is_sized`: its counts are callgrind's.
- **Callers.** Callgrind's caller edges are not reliable on this aarch64
  host. It reports `redecl` calling itself, for example. So the exact
  counts in tables (ii) and (iii) come from a temporary build.
- **The temporary build** put `#[track_caller]` counters on the queried
  functions and their `TypeContext` helpers. Each call is charged to the
  first frame outside those helpers. That build was not committed, so
  the release path has no counter.

**(i) Self Ir, M, and calls.** Calls are in millions, and include calls
that were inlined. "—" means under 0.1 M.

### Design note

**(a) Kind predicates (F8).**
- The `TypeContext` helpers stop building `TypeView`. These are:
  - `builtin`, `pointee`, `element`, `array_size`, `as_atomic`,
    `as_complex`, `as_record`, `as_enum`;
  - `is_integral`, `effective_quals`, and the predicates built on them.

  Each reads the canonical `TypeNode`, matches its kind, and decodes
  only the field it returns. Their decoding is the same as `view`'s.
- **New `is_error`.** `is_error(q)` is `canonical(q).ty() ==
  TypeId::ERROR`, like `is_void`: builtins are interned once.
  `is_error_type` (the largest single caller) becomes one load.
- **New `view_if`.** `view_if(t, kind)` decodes `t` only when the node
  has `kind`. It is for the callers that match a single variant of a
  full `view`: `type_pattern`, `vector_parts`, the oven's `BitInt`
  width, `ctad.rs:391`, `conv/qual.rs` and `deduce.rs:524`.
- **Evidence:** (ii). In the top 12 on both shapes, 20 of 24 callers ask
  for a kind or one field. The full-view callers (`ty_node`,
  `deduce.rs:846`) stay as they are.

**(b) First and definition bits (F7).**
- **No room for a group index.** `DeclNode` is 28 bytes, and only bits
  14 and 15 of `misc` are free. A `DeclId`-indexed side table of group
  ids would add 4 bytes per declaration.
- **Two cache bits instead**, each of which can only go from unset to set:
  - `FIRST` means `first(d) == d`. `RedeclGroup.first` never changes,
    and a declaration's redeclaration link is fixed when it is pushed.
    So `DeclArena::push` sets `FIRST` for every non-template declaration
    whose link has no `prev`. A template's first declaration is reached
    through its pattern, so `template_decl` and `detached_template_decl`
    set `FIRST` when the pattern has it and the pattern's link was set to
    this template. Concepts have no pattern and are always first.
  - `DEFINITION` means `definition(d) == Some(d)`. It is set at push for
    declarations without a redeclaration group, and by
    `Ast::set_definition` for the definition of a group. A group's
    definition is set at most once.
- `first` and `definition` test the bit first. One `get(d)` load replaces
  the payload dispatch.
- `push` clears both bits from the incoming `misc`, because
  `template_decl` copies its pattern's `misc`. `misc` is never printed
  as raw bits, so dumps do not change.
- **Caller fix.** `lookup.rs` `filter` keeps the `first` of each kept
  result next to it, so it calls `first` once per result instead of once
  per pair.
- **Evidence:** (iii). The median `d` is queried 5 to 256 times, and the
  hottest `d`s are first declarations: one ClassTemplate on nt, and the
  class templates and classes of tw and S8.

**(c) `template_redecl`.**
- The `FIRST` bit on a template answers `first(T)` without
  `template_info`, the pattern's `redecl` or `described_template`. On
  nt, S8 and aw, nearly all `template_redecl` calls come from `first`
  (`lookup`, `head_template`, `deduce.rs:708`, `deduce_call.rs:322`,
  `ics.rs:1349`).
- `latest(T)` keeps the round trip. Template `latest` calls are under
  0.1 M on every shape, and `latest`'s 0.3 M to 1.5 M calls are almost
  all `redecls_of` on non-templates.

**(d) `is_sized` (kiln_llvm).**
- `TypeTable` gets a parallel `Vec<Sized>` (1 byte per type), with three
  states:
  - `Yes`.
  - `No`, which is permanent: void, label, metadata, token, functions,
    and aggregates containing one of them.
  - `Pending`: an opaque named struct, or an aggregate containing one
    and nothing permanently unsized.
- The state is computed in `intern` from the elements' states.
- `set_body` computes the named struct's state from its fields.
- **Dependents do exist:** a literal struct, array or second named body
  that contains a struct before its body is set. So each `Pending` type is
  registered as a waiter on its `Pending` elements. When a struct
  resolves, a worklist re-evaluates its waiters.
- `is_sized` is `state == Yes`. That matches the walk, because the walk
  returns false exactly when it reaches an opaque struct or a permanently
  unsized type.
- **Test-only checks.** The recursive walk stays as a test-only
  reference. In debug builds, `TypeTable`'s drop checks every type
  against it.

**Not worth doing:**
- **Inline `TypeView` decoding into callers.** `view` is already one
  match, and the cost is the call count, which (a) cuts at the source.
- **A side table of group ids,** for the RSS above.
- **A `LATEST` bit.** `latest` changes with every redeclaration, so a bit
  could not stay correct.
- **Rewriting `redecls_of`** (1.15 M `redecl` on S8). It is `latest`
  plus one `prev` per step, and each step already costs a single
  payload load.
- **Speeding up `redecl` itself.** That would mean a second copy of the
  payload dispatch.
- **Caching `template_redecl` for `latest`,** for the reason in (c).

### Per-step Ir

Self Ir is counted the same way as in (i): the function itself plus its
inlined copies, by source line. Steps 2 and 3 are one commit, because
the template `FIRST` bit is (c). The aw column after step 4 is from the
branch head, so it includes steps 1 to 4.

| step | shape | measured | before (M) | after (M) | change | total Ir before → after |
|---|---|---|--:|--:|--:|---|
| 1 kind queries | tw | `view` + `canonical_view` | 331.7 | 85.4 | −74.3% | 3.00 G → 2.78 G (−7.4%) |
| 1 | S8 | `view` + `canonical_view` | 716.7 | 297.4 | −58.5% | 11.37 G → 10.97 G (−3.5%) |
| 1 | aw | `view` + `canonical_view` | 641.3 | 383.5 | −40.2% | 9.75 G → 9.50 G (−2.5%) |
| 2+3 bits, lookup | tw | `redecl` + `first` + `template_redecl` | 357.7 | 41.7 | −88.3% | 2.78 G → 2.57 G (−7.4%) |
| 2+3 | S8 | `redecl` + `first` + `template_redecl` | 947.2 | 152.6 | −83.9% | 10.97 G → 10.35 G (−5.7%) |
| 2+3 | nt (from main) | `redecl` + `first` + `template_redecl` | 892.0 | 192.3 | −78.4% | 8.64 G → 7.88 G (−8.8%) |
| 2+3 | aw | `redecl` + `first` + `template_redecl` | 754.8 | 177.8 | −76.4% | 9.50 G → 8.92 G (with step 4) |
| 4 `is_sized` | aw | `is_sized` | 158.7 | 1.3 | −99.2% | (above) |

Notes on the table:
- **`is_sized` after step 4 inlines to one load at each call site**, so
  no Ir is charged to its lines. The 1.3 M (119,646 calls) comes from a
  temporary build with `#[inline(never)]` that was not committed. Before
  step 4, the walk was about 1,300 Ir per top-level call.
- **Main to branch, total Ir:**
  - tw 3.00 G → 2.57 G (−14.3%);
  - td 0.726 G → 0.713 G (−1.9%);
  - nt 8.64 G → 7.88 G (−8.8%);
  - aw 9.75 G → 8.92 G (−8.5%);
  - S8 11.37 G → 10.35 G (−8.9%).
- **Targets.** `view` + `canonical_view` on tw: −74%, against a target
  of −40% or better. `redecl` + `first` + `template_redecl`: −88% on tw
  and −84% on S8, against −40% or better. `is_sized` on aw: 1.3 M,
  against ≤ 5 M.
- **Node sizes.** `DeclNode` stays 28 bytes: the two bits are the spare
  bits 14 and 15 of `misc`. `TypeNode` is unchanged. `TypeTable` gains
  1 byte per LLVM type and a map of waiters. The map holds entries only
  for types that are still waiting on an opaque struct.

### A/B

Main (090e033e4) and the branch (ed94a4617) were run alternating, 3
pairs, on a shared host at load 10 to 18. Each cell is that pair's
median of 5 runs of `/usr/bin/time -f '%e %M'`. Δ compares the medians
of the pairs. The `.o` cells are single runs, as in WP-654. The `.fs`
rows are `-fsyntax-only`, the `.ll` row is `-S -emit-llvm -o /dev/null`
and the `.o` rows are `-c -O2 -o /dev/null`. S7 and S8 are
`-fsyntax-only -freflection` on libstdc++; S8 is the generated shape at
N = 100. `churn-vector` uses the harness command line.
